DEC-034 | Governed client defaults | Per connector: token bucket (governor 0.10.4) from `ConnectorInfo.rate_limit`, priority gate for concurrency (interactive first), circuit breaker opens after 5 consecutive failures (5xx, 503, network) for 30 s with one half-open trial, `Retry-After` (seconds or HTTP date) pauses the whole connector; pauses up to 10 s are waited out, longer ones fail fast with `RateLimited`; 2 retries with jittered backoff; singleflight and cache only for GET; cache key = blake3(method + URL), credentials never part of it; TTL from `Cache-Control` else the connector default | per-request limits only | change `ConnectorPolicy::from_info` | governor-0.10.4 source; DESIGN D-6.2/3/5

DEC-184 | Username availability check for the sign-up form (design.md 5.2, 9 phase 0): what may it reveal and how is it throttled | New `Auth::username_available` (REST `POST /api/v1/auth/username-available`, server fn `username_available`) normalises like sign-up (trim + lowercase) and answers available / taken (+ up to 3 free numeric variants) / invalid(rule). It reveals whether a username exists; sign-up already does (it answers `username_taken`), so it answers only when a sign-up is possible for the caller: first account, open sign-up, or a valid invite token (else `signup_closed` / `invalid_invite`, checked before any lookup, and sign-up now also checks the invite before the name). Lookups are limited to 30 per minute per instance (`USERNAME_CHECKS_PER_MINUTE`, fixed window, in memory, `too_many_attempts` with retry seconds); format-invalid names need no lookup and are not counted. Per-client limits are not possible yet: the server has no trusted client address (no proxy config). Sign-up itself is not rate limited (it hashes the password first, so each attempt costs an argon2 run) | per-IP limit (needs trusted-proxy config), per-name limit (useless against enumeration), no endpoint (design asks for it), CAPTCHA (third-party) | change `USERNAME_CHECKS_PER_MINUTE` or the gate in `Auth::signup_gate` (crates/auth/src/lib.rs); remove the route and server fn | design.md 5.2 and 9; OWASP Authentication Cheat Sheet, "Authentication and Error Messages" (https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authentication_Cheat_Sheet.html)
DEC-185 | Sign-up / sign-in error codes per field and rule, and where the rules live (design.md 5.2 step 4) | Each rule has its own code: `username_too_short`, `username_too_long`, `username_space`, `username_invalid_chars`, `username_taken` (409), `display_name_too_long`, `password_too_short`, `password_too_long` (422), plus the existing `invalid_invite`, `signup_closed` (403), `invalid_credentials`, `totp_required`, `invalid_totp` (401), `too_many_attempts` (429). They replace `weak_password`, `invalid` and `conflict` for auth (design.md 5.2 named `invalid_username`, `invalid_display_name`, `weak_password`, `conflict`; the finer codes let the UI say which rule failed). The list, the code -> field mapping and the rules (3..=32 chars of a-z 0-9 _ . -, display name <= 100 chars, empty -> username, password 10..=1024 chars, normalisation trim + lowercase) live in `contracts::auth` (wasm-safe, already a `web` dependency); `auth` validates with it, `domain::user::validate_username` keeps its own copy as an invariant (domain may not depend on contracts, D-3) and a test asserts both agree. Sign-in stays non-enumerating: any wrong or malformed username or password is `invalid_credentials`. Checks run in field order (username, display name, password), one code per answer; the client checks the format rules itself for the error summary. No "too common password" or "passwords do not match" check: design.md 2.4 says "No other rules" and the form has a Show toggle instead of a repeat field | keep one `invalid_username` code with a rule field in ErrorDto (changes every ErrorDto literal), return all field errors at once (same), a common-password list (needs a licensed list; NIST SP 800-63B suggests one, could be added later as `password_too_common`) | edit `contracts::auth::AuthErrorCode` and `AuthError::code` | design.md 2.4, 5.2; NIST SP 800-63B, password verifier requirements (https://pages.nist.gov/800-63-4/sp800-63b.html)

DEC-189 | Sign-up / sign-in form details design.md 5.2 leaves open (UX phase 0, form discipline) | (1) Username rule errors show after the first blur (never for an empty field) or after a submit; a "taken" answer shows as soon as the debounced check returns (400 ms after typing stops, or at once on blur; one request per distinct normalised value, only when the local rules pass); after a submit every field updates its error live while the summary keeps the submitted state until the next submit. (7) Closed sign-up is explained when the availability check answers `signup_closed`/`invalid_invite` (form alert), not by a separate page, because ClientConfig does not carry the sign-up mode. DEC-199 | Where the i18n checks live, and what stays untranslated (UX phase 0, WP-i18n) | (1) No new crate: the skeleton `l10n` crate left by a stopped run is removed (KICKOFF layout: crates are exactly D-3). The parity, key-reference and hard-coded-text checks are repository checks in `xtask` (`xtask/src/locales.rs`, `xtask/tests/locales.rs`, AST from fluent-syntax 0.12.0, the version fluent-bundle 0.16 uses), next to the architecture and white-label checks; the locale list and number formatter shared by web, notify and api live in `contracts::locale` (wasm-safe, no workspace deps). (2) The hard-coded-text check scans `view!` markup of `web` and `ui` (text nodes and the values of aria-label, title, placeholder, alt, label, text, `*_label`); text assembled in Rust code outside markup is not detected (reviewed by hand in this package), and dynamic key families (`status-{..}`) must be listed in the test. (3) The dev-only gallery (`/dev/gallery`, feature `gallery`, not in production builds) stays English. (4) Untranslated on purpose: provider attribution and licence texts (their required wording, marked `lang="en"`), technical error details from channels (delivery log, marked `lang="en"`) and integrations (badge tooltip), REST API error `message` fields (developer-facing; the `code` is the contract and the web app translates it), the plain-text CSRF 403. (5) Removed unused keys: nav-companions, nav-sessions, nav-search, search-hint, companions-empty, companions-you, item-year, auth-hide, integrations-last | an `l10n` crate; checks as unit tests inside web and notify | move `xtask/src/locales.rs` | KICKOFF section 3; HANDOFF 4.1 (i18n ruling); https://docs.rs/fluent-syntax/0.12.0
DEC-200 | Locale-aware numbers and dates | Own small formatter for en and de. fluent-bundle 0.16.0 has no locale data for numbers (`FluentNumber::as_string` prints the plain value; `NUMBER()` options only pad digits) but offers `FluentBundle::set_formatter`, so each bundle (web and notify, also brand overrides, which are now Fluent resources) gets a formatter built on `contracts::locale::format_decimal`: CLDR latn symbols (en decimal `.` group `,`; de decimal `,` group `.`), grouping from four digits, at most three fraction digits, the `NUMBER()` digit options honoured; `add_builtins` registers `NUMBER()`. Dates use the CLDR medium date (en `MMM d, y` with month names from `month-short-N`; de `dd.MM.y`) and short time (en `h:mm a`; de `HH:mm`). Timestamps show in UTC with a "UTC" marker: there is no per-user time zone yet, and server and browser must render the same text for hydration. Fixes German "3.5" -> "3,5" in rating labels. Plurals use Fluent selectors with the CLDR categories (intl_pluralrules, already used by fluent-bundle) | icu4x (icu_decimal, icu_datetime; Unicode-3.0, allowed by deny.toml): not chosen now because it adds locale data and formatting code to the browser bundle for two locales (size not measured); a per-user time zone | replace `format_decimal` and `I18n::date/time` with icu4x when more locales or time zones are needed | https://docs.rs/fluent-bundle/0.16.0 (FluentBundle::set_formatter, add_builtins, types::FluentNumber); CLDR number symbols and date/time patterns for en and de (https://cldr.unicode.org, by-type charts "Numbers" and "Date/Time Patterns"); src/rust/deny.toml
DEC-201 | Which language a page, a notification and an email use; where the user chooses it | Pages: the signed-in user's choice (`users.locale`, migration 0013, NULL = automatic), else the browser's Accept-Language, else the brand's `default_locale`; signed-out visitors: Accept-Language, else the brand default. Notifications (inbox, channels, digests, test messages, emails): the recipient's choice, else the brand default; an inbox item keeps the language it was rendered in. The choice is "Automatic" plus every supported locale named in itself with its own `lang`: in the account menu (a menuitemradio group like the theme) and on the You hub (a segmented control); saving reloads the page so the server renders `<html lang>`, the texts and the hydration config in the new language. REST: `PUT /api/v1/auth/me/locale` with `{"locale": "de" | null}` (422 `invalid` for an unsupported locale) and `UserDto.locale`. Visitors cannot choose (no cookie) | the brand default before Accept-Language for signed-in users without a choice (the ruling names only the brand default as the fallback; the browser is the better signal, the brand default stays the last fallback); a settings page (design.md 6.12 puts the locale under Settings > Account, which does not exist yet); a cookie for visitors | change `web::i18n::effective_locale` and `notify::Engine::locale_for` | owner ruling DEC-171 / HANDOFF 4.1; design.md 6.12, 8 (Language)
DEC-202 | DEC-008 reconsidered (owner, HANDOFF 4.5): RustFS instead of versitygw as the S3-compatible dev/test service? | Switch to RustFS 1.0.4 (Apache-2.0: LICENSE, Cargo.toml, console and rc repos, Docker Hub image, nixpkgs asl20) in all three places. Dev shell: `pkgs.rustfs` from the locked nixpkgs (binary cache, flake.lock unchanged) plus `pkgs.curl`; `cargo xtask services up` starts it on 127.0.0.1:55433 with RUSTFS_VOLUMES=.dev/s3/data, RUSTFS_ADDRESS, RUSTFS_ACCESS_KEY=test, RUSTFS_SECRET_KEY=testsecret, RUSTFS_CONSOLE_ENABLE=false, RUSTFS_REGION=us-east-1, RUSTFS_SCANNER_SPEED=slowest, waits for GET /health = 200 and creates bucket `assets` with a `curl --aws-sigv4` PUT (200 or 409 accepted; 503 right after the first /health 200 is retried); an old versitygw tree (.dev/s3/iam) is wiped once. Compose: optional profile `s3` (rustfs/rustfs:1.0.4, named volume, console off) plus a one-shot `s3-init` running `rc bucket create --ignore-existing`, not a dependency of the app (fs backend stays the default). GitLab test job: service rustfs/rustfs:1.0.4 alias s3 (console left on so both exposed ports open), TEST_S3_* set and the same signed PUT with retries, so the S3 test runs in CI instead of skipping. Gate measured on the 2 vCPU / 4 GB host 2026-11-06: `cargo test -p assets --test s3` green twice in a row (bucket already there the second time); idle RSS 145 MB after 3.5 min, 123 MB after 8 min, 0.1 % CPU; lowercase access key `test` accepted; the data directory must exist before start ("VolumeNotFound" otherwise); a repeated bucket PUT answers 200, not 409. Not verified here: the compose profile and the CI job (no docker on the host, DEC-064) | keep versitygw 1.9.0 (lighter, plain files on disk); RustFS only in compose and CI with versitygw kept for xtask (research section 6); SeaweedFS; MinIO old tag (DEC-008) | restore `pkgs.versitygw` in flake.nix and the `versitygw ... posix` spawn in xtask services.rs (git history at 71399ec), drop the compose profile and the CI service; TEST_S3_* keeps the test backend-agnostic | docs/research/rustfs.md; github.com/rustfs/rustfs LICENSE and api.github.com/repos/rustfs/rustfs (release 1.0.4 of 2026-10-25); docs.rustfs.com/en/reference/cli and /reference/environment-variables; rustfs Dockerfile at tag 1.0.4 (/data and /logs owned by rustfs); rc 0.1.36 tested against the local server; nixpkgs 1d060086 rustfs 1.0.4
DEC-203 | How the UX phase 0 review findings (docs/reviews/2026-11-06-ux0-review.md) are carried out where the review left a choice | (1) Lock-out time: `ErrorDto.retry_after_secs` (omitted when unset) plus `Retry-After` on 429; server functions carry one string, so the web app encodes `<code>;retry_after=<secs>` (`web::api::encode_error`) and shows `auth-lockout-minutes` (minutes rounded up, Fluent plural); the availability check's throttle has its own status line `auth-check-throttled`, the code stays `too_many_attempts` (DEC-155, DEC-184). (2) Language names in the picker come from a static table `contracts::locale::locale_name` (not brand-overridable; `locale-name` keys removed). (3) The auth pages' brand mark is the product name as text until the logo slot is shown; legal links come from `[legal]` with `legal-<key>` labels and render only when the brand has any. (4) The account avatar inside its labelled button is decorative (`Avatar decorative`, initials drawn by CSS) so the button passes WCAG 2.5.3; named buttons start with their visible text ("Mark as read: X", "Share by link: X"). (5) Dialog keeps the opener in its effect's own state, never in a `StoredValue::new_local` (with one, signed-in pages ended with ERR_INCOMPLETE_CHUNKED_ENCODING and never hydrated in the e2e run; presumably the local value was dropped on another server thread, not verified further) | carry the seconds in a separate server-function error type; translate locale names in each catalogue again; draw a logo now; keep initials as text and drop the aria-label | revert the items above in `contracts`, `web/src/{api,auth,auth_pages,shell}.rs`, `ui/src/{avatar,dialog}.rs` | docs/reviews/2026-11-06-ux0-review.md; design.md 2.4, 3.2, 5.2, 6.14, 8; WCAG 2.2 SC 2.5.3 and 2.4.3; RFC 9110 section 10.2.3 (Retry-After); e2e runs of 2026-11-06
DEC-204 | Kind groups (UX phase 1, design.md 3.1): where the group lives and how the UI names it | `domain::kind::KindGroup` (`watch`, `read`, `listen`, `play`, `other`), a field of `KindDescriptor` (`#[serde(default)]`, unknown ids read as `other`). `KindModule::group()` defaults to `Other`; each built-in module (`screen`, `read`, `listen`, `play`) sets its group with one module constant, so a kind takes the group of its module. Labels are Fluent keys `kind-group-<id>` plus `kind-group-all` for the switcher, sent as `label_key` in `KindsDto` (`GET /kinds`, server fn `kinds`). `other` is listed only when a kind is in it | a free string id; groups only in the web app; a per-kind override list in the brand | drop the field and `group()`; the UI maps kind ids to groups itself | design.md 3.1, 9 (phase 1)
DEC-205 | The library query (design.md 3.5, 6.2): transport, matching and sorts | One query object `LibraryQueryDto` (all fields optional) sent as JSON: `POST /api/v1/library/query` and server fn `library_query` (lists do not fit plain query parameters; the page keeps its URL state and maps it to the object). Empty lists select everything; statuses, kinds and group combine with AND; tags: the entry carries all of them (case-insensitive); facets: `(attribute, value)` pairs, the attribute must be a descriptor facet of a selected kind (else 422 `invalid`), and the item attribute equals the value or, for a list, contains it, compared as text; text filter `q` goes through the search port's `library` index (title, original title, note, review) and at most the best 100 matches (the port's page cap) are intersected with the other filters. Sorts: `last_activity` (default: latest effective progress event, else when added; newest first), `title` (A to Z), `added`, `year`, `rating`, `plays` (logged sessions); missing years and ratings sort last in both directions; ties by entry id. Page 48 by default, at most 500. Not built: sorts Progress, Next release and Priority (no total per item for every kind, no release calendar, no priority field yet) | GET with repeated parameters; filters in the web app over a full library download; a separate search endpoint for the text filter | change `api::browse::library_query` and `LibraryRepo::query` | design.md 3.5, 6.2; search crate `filterable`/`searchable`
DEC-206 | Tags on entries (design.md 3.1, phase 1): storage, identity, privacy | Table `entry_tags (entry_id, user_id, tag, tag_key)` (migration 0014), reached through new `LibraryRepo` methods (`tags_of`, `set_tags`, `tag_counts`, `rename_tag`) so `app::Repos` keeps its shape. A tag is trimmed with inner spaces collapsed, 1 to 50 characters, at most 50 per entry; identity is case-insensitive (`tag_key` = lower case) and the form as typed is kept; lists show the form on the earliest added entry. Tagging an item adds it to the library. Rename onto an existing tag merges them. Tags are private like notes: they appear only in the owner's own answers, never on shared lists, shared sessions or public pages. A tag change records a `library_entry` change, so sync clients and the indexer notice. Not yet: tags in the sync commands, the export file and the search index | tags as a JSON column on `library_entries`; a tag table with ids and a join table; case-sensitive tags | drop `entry_tags`, remove the methods | design.md 3.1, 6.2, 6.3
DEC-207 | `app::next_step` (design.md 3.4) per progress model | Signature `next_step(entry, kind, item, progress)`: the catalog item is an extra argument because season data and totals live in its attributes. Single: `finish` with `again` once finished before or Completed. Episodic: the episode after the highest regular episode watched (season 0 specials ignored; rewatches of older episodes do not move it), rolling over with `attributes.seasons` (or `episode_count` as one season), `caught_up` when the last known episode is watched, episode + 1 when nothing is known. Paged: latest page/chapter with `page_count`/`chapter_count`. Timed: latest position with `duration_seconds`. Sessions: number of plays. Paused and Dropped get the same step as In progress; a Completed show is `caught_up` (rewatch is in the overflow menu). Labels (chosen in `api::browse::next_step`): Watch group "Watched"/"Watched again", other groups "Finished"/"Finished again", Listen episodes "Listened to", board games "Log a play", other session kinds "Log a session" (`next-step-*` keys, a listed dynamic family in the locale test). "Up to date" is the `caught_up` action; it does not know yet whether a show still airs | a label string from the server; the next step in the web app | change `app/src/next_step.rs` and the label table | design.md 3.4, 5.4
DEC-208 | Undo of progress (design.md 5.4, phase 1 "undo_progress") | `Progress::Undo { event_id }` is a compensating event appended by `App::undo_progress`; nothing is changed or deleted (D-12). Columns `progress_events.undoes` (unique) and `status_change` (migration 0014): every event stores the status move it caused (`from`, `to`, HLC stamp) and undo restores `from` only while the entry still carries exactly that write, so a status chosen by hand afterwards stays. Rules: own events only (someone else's answers `not_found`), within 24 hours of recording (`UNDO_WINDOW_HOURS`, else `conflict`), an undo cannot be undone, repeating an undo returns the same compensating event (client op id derived from the event). Undoing a `session` event also deletes its session (sessions are mutable records). Not undone: the "watched with" session logged next to a `finished` event, and the library entry an event created (it goes back to Planned). Sync clients see `{"type": "undo", "event_id": ...}` in the history; `LogProgress` refuses the undo type. `ItemPageDto.progress` is now the effective history | delete events; an `undone_at` column on the original event; a 10-second window matching the toast | revert `undo_progress`, keep the columns unused | design.md 5.4, 9; DESIGN.md D-12
DEC-209 | The private entry note (`entry_note` in design.md 9) | No new column: `library_entries.notes` already is a private per-entry field with HLC last-writer-wins, synced by `SetNotes` and shown only to its owner. The phase 1 API names it the entry note: `PUT /api/v1/library/{item_id}/note` (`NoteDto`) and server fn `set_entry_note`, server-stamped, blank clears it, at most 20 000 characters (as reviews); it stays `notes` in `LibraryEntryDto` | a second column `entry_note` next to `notes` | add the column and migrate `notes` into it | design.md 3.1, 6.3, 9; DEC-128
DEC-210 | Source and shape of the activity feed (Home, History) | Built from the domain records, not from the `user_changes` table: effective progress events (undone and undo events left out), the user's own sessions, and sessions linked users share under the existing privacy rule (`share_sessions`). The change feed has neither the user-supplied time of an event (History groups by when it happened) nor rows for sessions others share. Rows are `progress` or `session`; a "watched with" session within 120 s of a progress event of the same item becomes that row's people. Paging key `(time, id)`, cursor `<micros>_<uuid>`, newest first; filters group, kind, person (companion), row type, shared only, year (UTC). `undoable` marks own events still inside the undo window. Link requests, import results and "added to library" are not feed rows in phase 1 (they reach the inbox); sessions are read unpaged per request (fine at today's sizes) | rows from `user_changes`; a materialised activity table | change `app/src/activity.rs` | design.md 3.6, 6.1, 6.6; OD-03
DEC-211 | Statistics (owner ruling DEC-171: all-time totals per group on Home, series per group on /stats) | Computed per request in `app` from the effective history, no stored aggregates. Totals per group and overall: entries by status, events, completions, finished, episodes, pages, plays, minutes. Series per UTC month (`YYYY-MM`) or year (`YYYY`), buckets without activity left out. Minutes only where derivable: movie `runtime_minutes` per finish, show `episode_runtime_minutes` per episode, forward moves of timed positions, session `duration_minutes`; nothing for anime, podcasts or books. Completions over time are events that moved an entry to Completed; a status set by hand has no time and counts only in the totals. Plain numbers; the UI formats them. Not yet: per-person series and the user's time zone (there is no per-user time zone) | materialised counters; local-time buckets | change `app/src/stats.rs` | design.md 6.1, 6.6; DEC-171
DEC-212 | Home "Continue" and "Up next" | Continue: In progress entries by last activity, 6. DEC-233 | Item page layout and log sheet choices (design.md 6.3, 5.4, 5.5, 7.3; addendum-1 1.3) | Facts card: status (a select, changed in place), progress (the library's words, else the next step), rating; "More facts" (native details): added, started (first event), last logged, rewatches (finished minus one) or plays; owned flags wait for data. (sheet), overflow; paged = page field with Update, +10, +25, Finished; timed = position field (`h:mm:ss`, `mm:ss` or minutes) with Update, +15 min, Finished; sessions = "Log a play" / "Log a session" (sheet). DEC-235 | Home and the Library query at 50 000 entries (review UX1-1; DEC-210, DEC-211, DEC-212 said "per request" without a size) | Bounded per request: (1) "next in series" is one statement over the completed and planned entries of the kinds whose attribute schema has `series` and `series_position` (from the registry), grouped per series in SQL, at most `limit` item ids (`LibraryRepo::next_in_series`); Up next then reads at most `limit` series rows and `2 * limit` planned rows (before: `MAX_PAGE` = 500 full planned rows plus every completed entry and its catalog item; an old next part behind 500 newer planned entries was missed). (2) The library query no longer runs a per-entry aggregate over `progress_events`: `library_entries.last_event_at` and `plays` are kept by triggers (an appended event, an undo included, recomputes its entry; a new entry picks up earlier events), the tag and facet subqueries run only when those filters are set, and title and year are read from the catalog only when they are the sort key (migration 0015, index `(user_id, kind, status)`). (3) Statistics are summed in SQL per kind and UTC month (`ProgressRepo::month_amounts`, same rules as `app::month_amounts`, which the in-memory store uses; a Postgres test compares both on every kind of contribution); totals and series add up those rows. Still linear: the statistics statement scans the user's effective events, and the activity feed reads all own and shared sessions per request (one row per session, cheap; windowed session reads are the way back if it grows). M17 load test extended (`apps/server/tests/load.rs`, 50 000 movies, 5 000 finished events, 500 books in 50 series, 50 games with 500 plays), same 300 ms p95 budget, debug build on the 2-vCPU VM: see the numbers in the review disposition; with `LOAD_EVENTS=40000` stats totals p95 197 ms and Home p95 270 ms, inside the budget | stored aggregates per month; a materialised view; keyset paging for the deep page | revert migration 0015 and the two repository methods; the old statements are in git history | docs/reviews/2026-11-07-ux1-review.md UX1-1
DEC-236 | A replayed `client_op_id` on plays and "watched with" (review UX1-3; amends DEC-232) | The client op id is the idempotency key of the whole command: the session id is derived from it (`Uuid::new_v5(op, user id)`), so a replay finds the stored session and answers it with the existing event (one session, the same event id). For plays the event is appended before the session is stored; a replay of a play undone since (its session deleted) answers the undone event and stores nothing. The "watched with" path gets the same through the session id derived from its own derived op id. REST test `a_replayed_play_or_watched_with_stores_one_session`, app test `a_replayed_play_is_one_session_and_stays_gone_after_undo` | an op id column on `sessions` with a unique index | derive the id from `self.uuid()` again in `App::log_session_event` | docs/reviews/2026-11-07-ux1-review.md UX1-3

DEC-244 | Secrets at rest: algorithm, format, crates (OD-05 to OD-07 implemented) | AES-256-GCM for value and wrapped DEK, random 96-bit nonces from the OS, one DEK per value, format byte 0x01 + KEK version (u16 BE) at bytes 1-2, AAD `sb1|<purpose>|<row>|<field>` (`|dek` for the DEK); key file `v<n>:<base64>` lines newest first, then `APP__SECRETS__KEYS`, else generate `v1` with mode 0600; refuse to start when encrypted rows exist and no key is found; in-memory storage without a key uses a temporary key. Golden vector cross-checked with Node.js crypto (OpenSSL). DEC-245 | Not yet covered by secrets at rest | OIDC client secrets and the SMTP password still come from the environment only (no UI to store them yet, phase 3); the HKDF-derived OIDC pending-cookie key (purpose `cookie_key`) arrives with the OIDC work; the KEK operation counter (warn at 2^31) is not kept: the security page shows rows per version instead (each wrap is one row write). DEC-246 | Credential model (design.md 4.2) | Every compiled-in, enabled connector is registered (DEC-110 superseded); `catalog_core::CredentialState` (`not_needed`, `missing`, `env`, `db`, `invalid`, `locked`) lives in the registry (shared by clones), `KeySlot` per connector is read per request, so a key saved in the UI applies without restart; `invalid` = the provider answered 401/403 since the key last changed (from the governed client's counters), cleared by a new key. Environment keys win; a stored key is then shown as shadowed. Credential fields per connector (`CredentialSpec`): tmdb `api_key`; igdb `client_id`, `client_secret`; podcastindex `api_key`, `api_secret`; tvdb `api_key` and per-user `pin`; values are joined with `:` into the existing key strings. Per-user credentials are stored (encrypted, scope `user`) and listed, but the TVDB connector does not yet use the member's PIN for that member's requests (the connector context carries no user; follow-up) | keep registration only with a key | revert `default_registry` | design.md 4.2, 4.4
DEC-247 | Source status data (design.md 4.4) | `catalog_core::SourceStats` kept by the governed client: calls and 429s in hourly buckets for 24 h, last OK, last error (no secrets), last 401/403, circuit open until. In memory per process: with `--role web` and `--role worker` in separate processes the web role only sees its own calls (interactive search), the worker's background refreshes are not counted. Admin states `working`, `not_set_up`, `key_rejected`, `key_unavailable`, `rate_limited` (last 429 after the last OK), `circuit_open`, `disabled`; members see `working`, `not_set_up`, `needs_account` (hosted mode, per-user field not set), `unavailable`, `slow`, `retrying`. DEC-288 | Why did "a gated change asks to confirm it is you and is retried once confirmed" (src/web/tests/admin.spec.ts:74) lose "Saved." after the re-auth dialog? | Root cause: `settings::loaded` renders every new value of its resource afresh (UX1-2), and the save's `done` sets the message and then calls `data.refetch()`, so the fresh render of `Sessions` created a new, empty `message` signal; "Saved." lived only until the refetch answered, so the test passed or failed on timing (it passed in an earlier isolated run). The client address is the socket peer (`into_make_service_with_connect_info`); only when the peer is trusted, the first `X-Forwarded-For` address read from the right that is not itself a trusted proxy (an untrusted client cannot choose its address); IPv4-mapped IPv6 is folded to IPv4. The request id is a new UUIDv7, or a trusted proxy's `X-Request-Id` when it is 1-128 characters of `A-Za-z0-9-_.:`; every answer carries `X-Request-Id`. A middleware (`api::request_meta::layer`, on the REST and the page/server-function routers) keeps both in a tokio task-local, so `api::admin::entry` fills them for REST handlers and server functions alike instead of a field in `Caller` (most admin operations take only an `Actor`, and the web crate's `AdminBackend` passes a token only); `leptos_axum::handle_server_fns_with_context` runs the function in the request task (leptos_axum 0.8.10 source), so the task-local is visible. ID tokens are RS256, signed with `openidconnect`'s own `CoreRsaPrivateSigningKey` over a 2048-bit key from `rsa` 0.9.10 (MIT OR Apache-2.0, already in the tree through openidconnect; now a testkit dependency with `getrandom` and `sha2`), generated once per test process. HS256 would also work (openidconnect verifies HMAC ID tokens with the client secret for confidential clients, but only when the provider lists the algorithm; the default allowed set is RS256), so RS256 was chosen: it exercises the JWKS path real providers use. The environment's `auth.oidc` (new optional keys slug, scopes, groups_claim, allowed_groups, admin_groups, role_source) is upserted at start as the read-only row `ENV_PROVIDER_ID` with provenance env; its secret stays in memory, never stored; it is the default unless the admin chose another; removing `auth.oidc` deletes the row. DEC-310 | The pending-login cookie (addendum-1 4.2 "Redirect and callback", 2.2 `cookie_key`) | `v1.<base64url(json)>.<base64url(HMAC-SHA256)>`; the key is HKDF-SHA256 (RFC 5869, no salt, info `oidc-pending-cookie`) of the active KEK, derived on use and never stored; every loaded key version verifies, so a SIGHUP rotation during a sign-in does not break it. The JSON carries a kind (`login` or `link`, so one never passes for the other), the expiry (10 minutes) and provider id, issuer, state, nonce, PKCE verifier, return path and mode `interactive|silent|link|reauth` (+ the account for link/reauth). Single use: blake3(kind|state) is inserted into `oidc_used_states` (works across web processes; expired rows are deleted on each claim, no cron). Return paths must be local (`/`, not `//` or `/\`, no control characters), else `/`. The start route accepts `interactive` and `link`; `silent` and `reauth` answer 400 `mode_unavailable` and the callback refuses them until WP3-3 (start already adds `prompt=none` / `max_age=0` for them). Crates `hkdf` 0.13.0, `hmac` 0.13.0, `sha2` 0.11.1 (MIT OR Apache-2.0, already in the lock file); cargo-deny passes | blake3 keyed hash; a server-side pending table | change `auth::oidc::cookie` | RFC 6749 10.12; RFC 9700 4.2.4, 4.7.1; RFC 5869; docs.rs hkdf 0.13.0, hmac 0.13.0; addendum-1 2.2 table
DEC-311 | Discovery, mix-up defence and the code exchange (several providers) | Discovery is fetched through `HttpFetch` by us (`<issuer>/.well-known/openid-configuration`, the `issuer` member must equal the configured one exactly), then the JWKS; cached per process for 24 h, fetched again by Test and once when an ID token signature does not verify (key rotation); the document and its capabilities (end_session_endpoint, back-/front-channel logout, code challenge methods, RFC 9207 support, userinfo endpoint) are stored on the provider. PKCE S256 always; one redirect URI `<public_url>/api/v1/auth/oidc/callback`. Mix-up: the callback's `iss` must equal the pending issuer when present and must be present when the provider advertises `authorization_response_iss_parameter_supported`; otherwise `openidconnect` checks the ID token's `iss` against the discovered issuer. The provider in the cookie must still exist, be enabled and have the same issuer, else the generic failure. Failure codes (redirect `/sign-in?error=<code>`): `oidc_failed`, `oidc_denied`, `oidc_unavailable` (network, 5xx at discovery, `server_error`/`temporarily_unavailable` at the token endpoint or on the redirect), `group_rule`, `no_account`, `account_disabled`, `identity_in_use`; every callback stores `ok` or `failed: <code>` for the Overview. Groups come from the ID token, else from `UserInfo` (only when a rule or the `groups` scope needs them; JSON answers only, signed `UserInfo` JWTs are not supported; its `sub` must match) | discovery through `openidconnect::discover_async` (no access to the raw document); distinct redirect URIs per provider | change `auth::oidc::Oidc::discover`/`finish` | OpenID Connect Discovery 1.0 4.3; Core 3.1.3.7, 5.3.2; RFC 9207 2.4; RFC 9700 4.4.2; RFC 7636; RFC 6749 4.1.2.1; docs.rs openidconnect 4.1.0

Link mode does not apply provider roles. 3: a verified email (`email_verified: true`, compared case-insensitively; the oldest account when several match) leads to `/sign-in/link` with a signed 10-minute ticket cookie (`<prefix>_oidc_link`); `GET /auth/oidc/link` names the account and provider, `POST /auth/oidc/link {password, totp}` runs the normal throttled sign-in of that account (TOTP when enrolled) and links; the ticket is claimed after the password passed (a lost claim signs the new session out). An account without a password gets `has_password: false` (page: ask the admin); the emailed one-time link is phase 4 (plan). 4: a new account when it is the first, the provider has auto_create, or sign-up is open; username = sanitised `preferred_username` (`check_username`), `2`, `3`, ... on collision, else `user-<hash>`; display name from `name`; the email is kept only when verified. Saving a rule that would demote the acting admin (their last sign-in through this provider lacked an admin group) is refused with 409 `self_demotion`. Users: `AdminUserDto.role_via_provider` names the managing provider and `PUT /admin/users/{id}/role` answers 409 `role_managed_by_provider`. DEC-314 | Admin API for providers (addendum-1 4.2, 3.4; DEC-191, DEC-245) | `GET /admin/oidc` (providers, `redirect_uri`, `post_logout_redirect_uri`, `backchannel_logout_uri`), `POST /admin/oidc/providers` (201; `client_secret` read on create only), `PUT/DELETE /admin/oidc/providers/{id}`, `PUT /admin/oidc/providers/{id}/secret {secret|null}` (write-only), `POST /admin/oidc/providers/{id}/test`; public `GET /auth/oidc/providers` for the sign-in buttons. Create, change, secret and delete are re-auth gated and audited in the change's transaction (`oidc_provider.created/updated/deleted` with a field summary, before/after and the proof; `secret.replaced/cleared` with purpose `oidc_client_secret` and key version, never the value). DEC-319 | Back-channel logout endpoint (addendum-1 4.2, A-21) | `POST /api/v1/auth/oidc/backchannel-logout`, form field `logout_token` (Back-Channel Logout 1.0, 2.5); public, 60 requests per minute and process (429 beyond), `Cache-Control: no-store`; 200 when valid (also when no session matched), 400 `{"error":"invalid_request"}` otherwise (2.8), 500 on storage errors. Validation (2.6): a JWS with an asymmetric `alg` (HS* and `none` refused), `typ` absent or `logout+jwt`/`JWT`; the enabled provider whose issuer equals `iss` and whose client id is in `aud`; signature against its JWKS (by `kid` when given; one forced discovery on a miss for key rotation); `exp` in the future, `iat` at most 60 s ahead and at most 10 minutes old (so a token older than the replay cache is refused by age); `events` has the member `http://schemas.openid.net/event/backchannel-logout` with an object value; no `nonce`; `sub` or `sid`; `jti` required and refused when seen within 10 minutes (per provider, in memory per process, at most 10 000 kept). Ending (2.7): with `sid` the sessions of that provider and OP session id; else every session of the (iss, sub) identity's account signed in through that provider (password sessions stay). Audit row `oidc.backchannel_logout` (actor system, target the provider; provider slug, `sessions`, `by: sid or sub`). DEC-371 | UX3-9, UX3-20: reading `X-Forwarded-For` behind trusted proxies; mapped IPv6 networks | The chain (all header lines, in order) is walked from the right starting at the trusted peer: a trusted entry is skipped and remembered as the last good hop, the first untrusted entry is the client, a malformed entry stops the walk and the last good hop is recorded (so a client-sent `x` left of the real entry cannot hide the client, and a bad entry never makes the peer win over a better hop); when every entry is trusted the leftmost one reached is recorded (an internal client inside the trusted range). `ip:port` and `[v6]:port` entries are read. A trusted network written as IPv4-mapped IPv6 (`::ffff:10.0.0.0/104`) folds to IPv4 with its prefix less 96 in the server, and config validation accepts only 96..=128 for such networks, so both agree. DEC-384 | Why a server-side render hung for minutes at 0% CPU under full CI load (orchestrator item 0; review UX3-25) | Root cause: `leptos_axum::generate_route_list` -> `leptos_router::RouteList::generate` renders the app once with `leptos_server::SuppressResourceLoad` held, which sets the process-wide `IS_SUPPRESSING_RESOURCE_LOAD` (leptos_server 0.8.8 `resource.rs:37-61`, leptos_router 0.8.17 `generate_route_list.rs:237`); every `Resource` whose fetch is first polled anywhere in the process while the flag is set awaits `futures::future::pending()` forever (`resource.rs:317`). `Server::app_router` listed the routes on every call and every server test builds its own router, so a page rendered by one test while another built its router never finished: the app-level `current_user` resource exists on every page, so `/you`, the sign-in pages, the wizard and the item page all hung this way (all threads parked, no lock held: a never-woken future). Production builds one router before listening and was not affected. Fix: `server::page_routes()` lists the routes once per process (`OnceLock`), before the first router exists. Evidence: before the fix, 18 server test binaries run in parallel for 7 rounds hung 6 times (auth_pages x3, setup_page, shell, item_page; core dumps of auth_pages show every thread parked in tokio/condvar waits, including a test on a current_thread runtime); the new test `ssr_routes::pages_render_while_other_routers_are_built` (routers built in a loop while signed-out pages render) failed first with 3 of 14 pages never finishing and passes after the fix; after the fix the same parallel stress ran 5 rounds (90 binary runs, with a workspace clippy build running alongside) without a hang. Fail fast: every server test request now goes through `tests/support::send` (answer and whole body within 90 s, else a panic naming method and path). UX3-25 (a "reactive value disposed" panic in leptos `transition.rs`) was not reproduced. Its only `transition.rs` path here is the `set_pending` effect of the Library results `Transition`, which may run once more while an aborted stream tears the page down. A `SignalSetter::map` over `try_set` was tried and reverted: the mapped closure runs while the setter holds the arena read lock, and the nested read deadlocked against a waiting writer (`library_page` stalled at 0% CPU, core dump). It stays open for phase 4, to be re-checked with the server log of an e2e run now that the hang is gone. History's remounting boundary (UX3-3, the review's other candidate) is fixed. DEC-400 | WP4-1: incremental statistics totals (DEC-235) | Migration `0023_stats_cache.sql`: `stats_item_months (user_id, item_id, kind, month, events, completions, finished, episodes, pages, plays, seconds)` keyed by (user, item, month) in the user's zone, without a foreign key on the user (a 40k-event rebuild took 778 ms with the per-row check and 337 ms without, EXPLAIN ANALYZE; the rows go with the user's `stats_users` row through a delete trigger); `stats_months (user_id, kind, month, ...)`, the sums per kind and month that a read returns (the rollup the plan named as the fallback: with sums over the item rows the 40k/5k ratio of the totals p95 was 2.0, target 1.5); `stats_dirty (user_id, item_id)`; `stats_users (user_id, zone)` as the zone stamp. Triggers mark pairs dirty: progress events after INSERT (statement level with a transition table, so a bulk import marks each pair once; an undo also marks the undone event's item), UPDATE and DELETE; sessions after INSERT, DELETE and an UPDATE that changes the duration, item or owner (marks owner and session item); `catalog_items` after an UPDATE of `kind`, `runtime_minutes` or `episode_runtime_minutes` (marks every user with events on the item, through the new index `progress_events (item_id)`). Delete triggers skip users already gone (cascade). `ProgressRepo::month_amounts` keeps its signature: a read checks stamp and dirty set; if clean it returns the `stats_months` rows; else, in one transaction holding the user's `stats_users` row (`FOR UPDATE`, so concurrent readers wait for one rebuild), it takes the dirty set with `DELETE ... RETURNING` before reading events (a write committing later stays marked) and recomputes those items with the old statement restricted to `item_id = ANY(...)`, and keeps `stats_months` by subtracting the removed item rows and adding the new ones (each aggregated per kind and month, upserts), dropping months left at zero events, so no month index is needed (one cost the rebuild 125 ms); everything is rebuilt when the stamp is another zone. `stats_users.bucket_zone` remembers the zone Postgres buckets in (UTC when its tzdata lacks the name, UX3-20): the `pg_timezone_names` check reads the tz directory (47 ms measured) and now runs once per zone change, not on every refresh. The rebuild sets `work_mem` to 32 MB for its sort and joins session durations instead of a per-event subquery. The full statement stays as `PgStore::month_amounts_full` (tests compare against it). Not built, by design: a `users.time_zone` trigger (the stamp compares the zone name on every read, which also covers the UTC fallback of UX3-20) and a trigger on new library entries (the statistics do not read entries; the test removes and re-adds an entry). A session's duration is marked for the session's own item, not for events of other items that name the session (the app never writes those). The in-memory store keeps computing from scratch. FOR UPDATE"
DEC-401 | WP4-1: paged activity feed (DEC-235) | `SessionRepo::by_owner_window` and `with_participants_window` take a `HistoryWindow` (strictly before `(started_at, id)`, at or after `since`, item kind in `kinds`, `LIMIT`); Postgres orders by `(started_at DESC, id DESC)` on the new index `sessions_owner_recent (owner_id, started_at DESC, id DESC)` (replaces `sessions_owner`). `activity_feed` reads events, own sessions and shared sessions in the same window, `2 * limit + 1` rows each, doubling per window while the page is not full (at most 2 000), never all sessions. The window's lower end is the newest last key among the sources that filled their page. A "watched with" pair split by a window end is matched as in one big read: the pairing also reads own sessions and events within 120 s above and below the window (at most 64 each, never past the year bounds); the old feed lost such pairs between pages (the new test failed on the old code in pages of 1). The job looks as a system admin actor (nil user id) so the data-source row uses the admin vocabulary and no member credential. Every process runs the probe once in `Server::build` (so a fresh instance and the test harnesses have rows before the first tick). DEC-406 | WP4-2: data-source counters shared across processes (DEC-247, DEC-326) | `catalog_core::SourceStats` records into a pending map; `SourceStats::sync(&dyn StatsStore)` (every `STATS_SYNC_SECS` = 60 s in every role, `Server::run_stats_sync` spawned by `main`, a last flush on shutdown, one read in `Server::build`) adds the pending deltas to Postgres and reloads the last 24 hours; a status read is snapshot + deltas in flight + pending, so nothing is counted twice and a failed write is retried. Tables (`0024`): `source_stats_hours (connector, hour, calls, rate_limited)` added up (rows older than 48 h dropped by the flush) and `source_stats (connector, last_ok_at, last_error_at, last_error, last_auth_status, last_auth_failure_at, last_rate_limited_at, circuit_opened_at, circuit_open_until, key_changed_at)` where each mark keeps the newer value (`GREATEST`; a text/status/until goes with its time), so flushes merge in any order. Two schema changes from the plan's single table: hours and marks are separate (one row per hour vs one per connector), and the marks gained `last_rate_limited_at` (the `rate_limited` state needs it), `circuit_opened_at` (an OK after the opening closes the circuit) and `key_changed_at` (a rejection counts only when newer than the last key change; this replaces clearing in memory, so a key replaced in the web process clears the worker's rejection after the next flush). `Registry::set_key` still marks a key change at start-up, so a restart forgets an older rejection of the same key in every process after the flush, as a restart did before. `PgSourceStats` lives in `catalog_core::pg` (the crate already uses sqlx). Test: `catalog-core/tests/stats_shared.rs` (web and worker `SourceStats` on one database: the worker's 429 shows on the web role, no double counting, rejection and key change across processes, open circuit) | a table written per call; a single table | `SourceStats` back to the in-memory version, drop the sync loop and the two tables | DEC-247, DEC-326; phase4-plan.md WP4-2

Test: `apps/server/tests/oidc.rs` `back_channel_jti_replay_through_a_second_process_is_refused` (two servers on one database with a fixed `APP__SECRETS__KEYS`; seen failing with 200 before the change). DEC-413 | WP4-2: the emailed one-time link of "Is this your account?" (DEC-312, DEC-316; addendum-1 4.2 step 3) | `GET /auth/oidc/link` adds `can_email` (no password, a stored address, and mail works: settings in effect and a transport). `POST /auth/oidc/link/email` (ticket cookie) makes `lnk_<24 random bytes>`, stores only its hash in `password_resets` with the new columns `purpose = 'oidc_link'` and `binding` (blake3 of the ticket's single-use id), 30 minutes (`EMAIL_LINK_MINUTES`), earlier open links of the account revoked; renews the ticket cookie to the same 30 minutes (the ticket alone lasts 10); sends `sign_in_link` (notify templates en/de with provider, username, minutes, URL `<public_url>/sign-in/link?code=...`, in the account's language) to the stored address; answers 202 with the address partly hidden (`p***@example.test`); audited `auth.link_emailed`; throttled to 3 links per account per 15 minutes per process (429 `too_many_attempts`); 409 `email_unavailable` without mail or with a password, 503 `mail_failed` when sending fails. "The stored verified email": a password-less account only ever gets its address from a verified provider claim (step 4 keeps only `email_verified` addresses), so no separate flag is stored. `POST /auth/oidc/link/email/confirm {code}` needs the same browser's ticket (else 400 `link_expired`), claims the code for that binding once (else 400 `code_invalid`: unknown, used, expired or another browser's ticket), refuses disabled accounts, claims the ticket once, links the identity and signs in through the provider (session method `oidc`, provider and `sid` kept for back-channel logout); audited `auth.link_confirmed`. Password resets now only use rows with `purpose = 'password'`, so a link code never resets a password. The page `/sign-in/link` offers "Email me a link" instead of "ask your admin" when `can_email`, and with `?code=` confirms at once in this browser ("Linking your account..."), showing `signin-link-code-invalid` or the expired ticket otherwise. The notify template test accepts `sign_in_link` as a transactional message next to `digest` and `test`. E2E: `tests/mail-sink.mjs` (a plain SMTP listener with an HTTP read side, written from RFC 5321) is a Playwright web server on 3013/3014. Tests: `oidc.rs` `emailed_link_links_a_passwordless_account_once` (seen failing first; no offer without mail, hash only, never a reset, other browser and other ticket refused, once, expiry, throttle), `signin.spec.ts` emailed link through the mail sink | a magic link that signs in anywhere; a 10-minute link; a separate table | drop the two routes, `can_email` and the columns | addendum-1 4.2 step 3; DEC-312; DEC-316; RFC 5321 (mail sink)

DEC-416 | Keyset paging for the library (added to WP4-3 by the orchestrator; DEC-404: "library, last 100" sat at p95 297/306 ms under 8 clients + 2 writers because the page used `OFFSET 49 900`) | Both library reads page by cursor instead of `OFFSET`. `GET /library?after=&limit=` and `POST /library/query {.., after}` answer `next` (absent on the last page); `offset` is gone from the requests and answers (`LibraryPageDto`, `LibraryRowsDto`, `LibraryQueryDto`; greenfield, no client used deep offsets). A cursor (`app::ports::LibraryCursor`: sort, direction, the last row's key and entry id) travels as opaque URL-safe base64 of JSON (`app/src/cursor.rs`); a malformed one is 400, one made for another sort or direction 422. The page read is `added_at <= $c AND (added_at < $c OR id < $id) ORDER BY added_at DESC, id DESC LIMIT n+1` on `library_entries_recent` (the extra row says whether more follow). The query statement stays one static statement: the sort picks one of three key columns (lower-case title text, a time for added/last activity, a number for year/rating/plays; the others stay NULL), the rows are ordered by that key and the id in the chosen direction with missing numbers last in both directions (as before), the cursor keeps rows strictly after (key, id), and the total is an uncorrelated `COUNT(*)` over the same CTE (an empty page after a cursor counts again without one). `GET /library` also reads its items in one `get_many` instead of one catalog read per entry (100 queries per page before). The web Library keeps "Load more" (48 more rows each time, the shown count still outside the URL) but loads the rows as one request up to 500 and then follows `next` in blocks of 500, so it no longer stops at the 500-row clamp. The load harness walks the pages once (500 rows a request) to get the cursors of the last 100 rows of both reads, after one logged page has moved the writers' book to the top (it would leave the last page meanwhile). Release, 8 clients + 2 writers, data as DEC-399 (b), p95 ms before (one run on HEAD before the change, from a `git archive`) -> after (two runs): library first 100: 223 -> 29 / 27; library last 100: 310 (the budget assert failed again) -> 27 / 31; library query first 100: 215 -> 231 / 202; library query last 100: 247 -> 146 / 146; the other endpoints within noise (Home 147 -> 136 / 143, search 209 -> 214 / 216, person 205 -> 217 / 205). Deep pages now cost what the first page costs; the plain page's first-page gain is the batched item read; the query's remaining cost is the full filtered scan for the count and the last-activity key | keep `OFFSET` with the batched item read only (the deep page would still read 49 900 index rows); keyset only on the plain page; drop the total on later pages (the UI shows "Showing N of M") | restore `OFFSET` in `store-pg/src/repos/library_page.rs` and the `offset` fields, and the `load_rows` loop in `web/src/library.rs` | DEC-235, DEC-399, DEC-404; PostgreSQL 17 docs 7.6 "LIMIT and OFFSET" (rows skipped by OFFSET still have to be computed) and "Row and Array Comparisons: Row Constructor Comparison"
DEC-417 | WP4-3: remove from library and bulk edits with one undo (design.md 3.5, 6.2, 6.3; DEC-348 "delete keeps history") | `App::remove_from_library` (= a bulk `remove` of one id) deletes the entry and its tags only (`LibraryRepo::remove_many`, one transaction, outbox `delete` rows so search and sync follow); progress events, sessions, reviews and list memberships stay, so statistics keep counting the history and a re-add shows it again (the existing insert trigger fills `last_event_at`/`plays`). `App::bulk(actor, ids, op)` with `BulkOp::{SetStatus, AddTag, RemoveTag, AddToList, Remove}`: at most `MAX_BULK` = 500 ids (REST answers 400 above; the app refuses too), the same id twice counts once, one batched read of the entries (`LibraryRepo::entries_for`) and tags, entry writes in one transaction (`LibraryRepo::write_many`, entry plus optional tags), removals in one, the list in one; each id fails alone (`not_in_library` for unknown or someone else's ids, `status_not_allowed`, `too_many_tags`); an invalid tag or a list of someone else fails the request. One `entity.updated` event per request (one per entry would refetch open pages up to 500 times). The undo is a snapshot of every changed entry before the change (entry id, item, status, rating, note, `added_at`, tags) plus the items a list gained, returned to the client as an opaque token (URL-safe base64 of JSON) - the plan's "snapshot returned to the client"; `App::undo_bulk` checks it like any input (item exists, the kind has the status, rating range, tags normalised, at most 500) and only ever writes into the caller's own library with fresh stamps (so the restore wins LWW); a removed entry comes back with its id and `added_at` unless that id is taken meanwhile; the list loses only the items the request added. A forged token can only set values the caller could set anyway. Undoing a status change also re-stamps rating and note with their old values. REST: `POST /library/bulk {item_ids, op, status?, tag?, list_id?}` -> `{results[{item_id, ok, error}], done, undo}`, `POST /library/bulk/undo {undo}` (204), `DELETE /library/{item_id}` (same answer, 404 when not in the library); server functions `library_bulk`, `library_bulk_undo`. Tests: `app/tests/bulk.rs` (remove keeps events, stats and list membership, re-add, undo restores id/place/status/rating/note/tags; mixed ids with a foreign id failing alone for every op; undo per op; >500 refused; token round trip and replay by another user), `store-pg/tests/phase1.rs` `remove_keeps_history_and_bulk_undo_on_postgres`, `apps/server/tests/library_page.rs` `bulk_edits_500_items_and_refuses_more` | keep undo snapshots in a server table with expiry (smaller token, but another table and cleanup); a soft-delete flag on the entry (every library read would have to filter it); one event per entry | drop `bulk.rs` (app, api, web), the three routes and the two repo methods | design.md 3.5, 6.2, 6.3; DEC-348; phase4-plan.md WP4-3

An intent while a flow runs counts as a tap of that flow (palette -> item page -> next step is one palette flow); a flow untouched for 2 minutes is abandoned. Taps = clicks while the flow runs (document `click` listener in the capture phase, the intent's own click is the first tap), buckets `1..4`, `5+`; time from the intent to the end, buckets `lt2s, lt5s, lt10s, lt30s, lt60s, 60s+`. DEC-448 | WP4-7: where, when and how often episodes are fetched and stored | A detail refresh (`catalog_core::refresh::refresh_item`, any lane) that finds an episode source queues `catalog.episodes` (`app::EPISODES_JOB`) on `background_refresh`, deduplicated per item, only when a season needs it; the job (`refresh_episodes`, handler in apps/server) asks the source once per such season through the governed HTTP client and its cache (op `season`). Why a second job: page views queue the detail refresh on the interactive lane (`App::item_detail`), so a fetch limited to "the detail-refresh job on the background lane" inline would almost never run; the plan's intent (one call per season, never in an interactive request, background lane) is kept. A season is asked again only when nothing is stored for it, its `episode_count` or `air_date` in the season list changed, fewer episodes are stored than it has, or one of its episodes has no air date or airs today or later (dates still to come are corrected by the provider); a failed season (404, network) is logged, not stored, and asked again at the next refresh (at most weekly per viewed item, `FRESH_FOR_SECS`). Source: the connector that supplied `attributes.seasons` (provenance), else the first with `Capability::Episodes` and an external id. Storage (migration `0027_catalog_episodes.sql`): `catalog_episode_seasons (item_id, season, connector, episode_count, air_date, fetched_at)` records what each season was fetched for (the plan names only `catalog_episodes`; the fingerprint needs its own row because a season can have no episodes yet) and `catalog_episodes (item_id, season, number, air_date, runtime_minutes)`, no titles or overviews; a fetched season replaces its rows in one transaction; both cascade with the catalog item. Ports: `CatalogRepo::episode_seasons/put_episode_seasons` (existing port, as DEC-442). `App::store_episodes` publishes `entity.updated` once, only when episodes changed. `SeasonInfo` gained optional `air_date` (schemas regenerated). The item page carries `ItemPageDto.episodes` (`season`, `episode`, `air_date`) | fetch inline in every detail refresh; refresh only on the background lane; one table with a season-level sentinel row | drop the job, the tables and `ItemPageDto.episodes` | phase4-plan.md WP4-7; DEC-363; app/src/catalog.rs `item_detail`
DEC-449 | WP4-7: "On release dates" in the episode list (DEC-363 follow-up) | `BulkDates` gains a third option "On release dates", shown only when the item has release dates and every open episode of the season shown has one up to today (today counts as released; dates as noon UTC like the other backfill modes); otherwise, for an item with dates, a hint says how many open episodes of the season have none ("ep-release-missing"), and a chosen "On release dates" falls back to Now when the season changes. A bulk mark (season, show, up to here) on release dates logs each episode on its day; if any chosen episode has no release day up to today (missing or still to come) nothing is logged and an inline alert names the count ("future episodes refused"). Items without any dates show no option and no hint. Strings in en and de | offer it per item and refuse at marking time only; disable the radio instead of hiding it | drop the option in `BulkDates` and `release_dates` | design.md 3.4, 5.4 R-09; DEC-363; phase4-plan.md WP4-7

DEC-452 | WP4-8 item A (DEC-445): concurrent imports and lookups of one external id created a second catalog item | Cause: every creator (`upsert_catalog_item`, `ensure_catalog_item`, `find_or_create_catalog_item`; imports, webhooks, search results, connector data) looked the id up and, finding nothing, inserted a new item; `external_ids` already had its primary key `(kind, namespace, value)`, so the loser's id insert hit `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and the loser stayed as an item without any external id (never refreshed, so "one without seasons"). Fix in the database: new port `CatalogRepo::insert_new` inserts the item and claims its ids (sorted, so two inserters never wait on each other in opposite order) in one transaction with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING`; the unique index makes a concurrent claim wait for the other transaction; if any id is held by another item the transaction rolls back and that item's id is returned. The app then treats the winner like an item found by the lookup: connector data is merged into it (`Merge`), a search result only adds missing ids and cover (`Fill`), data from users leaves it unchanged (`Keep`, G2-2). The in-memory store does the same under its lock. Existing duplicates are not migrated: the loser of an old race carries no external id, so it cannot be told apart from an item created without ids (generic import rows, webhooks) except by title, and merging by title would move people's history between items on a guess; e2e and test databases are created fresh. An operator can list candidates with `SELECT c.id, c.title FROM catalog_items c WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM external_ids e WHERE e.item_id = c.id)`. Test: `store-pg/tests/catalog_race.rs` (16 concurrent creators through the three entry points -> one item, every caller gets it; a lost race from user data attaches nothing), seen failing first (14 distinct items, 4 rows), then green in 8 repeated runs | an advisory lock per external id (also race-free, but needs one lock per id and a second round trip); a unique constraint on title | let the app insert with `upsert` again (`crates/app/src/catalog.rs` `put_catalog_item`) and drop `insert_new` | DEC-445; PostgreSQL 17 docs "INSERT ... ON CONFLICT" (speculative insertion waits for a conflicting in-progress transaction)
DEC-453 | WP4-8 item B: TMDB terms 1.C ("Cache, for longer than 6 months, any information obtained through or from TMDB") while items nobody opens are never refreshed (DEC-446, 450) | Migration `0028_catalog_retention.sql`: `catalog_items.provider_data_at` = when the connector data was obtained (set on insert from the app's clock or `fetched_at`, moved only when `fetched_at` changes, i.e. a successful refresh; a failed refresh or a cover change does not move it; backfilled from `COALESCE(fetched_at, updated_at)`; partial index). `catalog_core::retention::sweep` (cron `catalog.retention`, `41 * * * *`, lane `background_refresh`) takes up to 500 items with an external id in a cache-limited namespace (`CACHE_LIMITED = ["tmdb"]`) whose data is older than `jobs.catalog_max_age_days` (new setting, default 150, 1..=170, `APP__JOBS__CATALOG_MAX_AGE_DAYS`), oldest first. If a usable details connector exists for each such id (key present, source not switched off) and the data is younger than `HARD_LIMIT_DAYS` = 175, it queues the ordinary `catalog.refresh` (deduped per item, so the governed client's rate limit and lane apply) and the server handler queues a new download of the stored cover (`AssetService::refetch`); otherwise `App::purge_provider_data` drops description, original title, attributes (genres, runtimes, seasons), cover URL and cover asset, provenance, per-episode dates and fetch times, makes the item a stub and clears the stamp, and the handler deletes the stored cover image (`AssetService::forget`: every variant and the original; the record stays so the URL can be fetched again). Title, year and external ids stay: they are the person's own record in their library and let a later view refresh the item; this keeps a TMDB title and year past six months for items whose refresh is impossible - a judgement left for the owner (alternative: blank titles, which breaks libraries). The same run deletes HTTP cache entries stale for longer than the setting and the bodies only they used (`HttpCache::purge_stale_before`). A purge drops runtimes, so statistics minutes of such items fall to zero until a refresh (the WP4-1 dirty trigger fires). Other connectors: no doc in `docs/connectors/` states an age limit (BGG: cache and purge on revocation; IGDB: caching preferred; Trakt: importer only), so only `tmdb` is listed; docs/connectors/tmdb.md "Caching" describes the job. Known limits: if TMDB fails but another connector refreshes the item, `fetched_at` moves although merged TMDB fields may be older (the merge keeps old values for fields no snapshot sent); a re-downloaded cover with new bytes leaves the old variants to the storage budget's eviction. Tests: `catalog-core/tests/retention.rs` (fake clock: refresh queued only for old TMDB data while a key exists, then young again after a refresh; purge without a key and not picked again; purge past 175 days even with a key; HTTP cache purge in memory and on Postgres), `store-pg/tests/retention.rs` (stamps, purge keeps title/year/ids, episodes go), `assets/tests/service.rs` (`forget`, `refetch`), `apps/server/tests/retention.rs` (cron hourly on lane 2, the worker purges old data with no TMDB connector), config unit test. Honest note: the retention tests could not compile before the module existed and passed on their first run (not seen red as assertions); the config test was seen failing (no validation) before the bound was added | purge only, never refresh (loses data an admin's key could renew); refresh on page view only (status quo, violates 1.C for unviewed items); delete unviewed items outright (they hold people's history) | drop the cron entry in `apps/server/src/handlers.rs` and the 0028 column | https://www.themoviedb.org/api-terms-of-use 1.C (read 2026-11-08 by WP4-7, DEC-446); docs/connectors/*.md

Test: `admin.spec.ts` "an admin save counts the open Invites disclosure as open at a save" (Admin > Users, open "Invites" (`app-disclosure:users:invites`), create an invite through `gated`; the intercepted `POST /api/v1/usage/funnel` beacon carries `{flow: disclosure, subject: users/invites, outcome: open_at_save}` and `opened`). DEC-455 | WP4-8 item D: can the send-time stamps of DEC-434 lose a newer edit from another device? | Yes: a status or rating changed offline on the phone at 10:00 and changed again on the laptop at 12:00 was overwritten when the phone synced at 18:00, because DEC-434 restamped field changes when they were sent; a queue left for days overwrote everything done meanwhile. Fixed: every queued command keeps the stamp it got in the queue, and the flush is one push in the order queued (`web::offline::flush_batch`, replacing `log_batch`/`field_batch`). The problem DEC-434 solved (the person's own queued log, applied at server time, beat the status they queued after it) is solved on the server instead: `SyncCommandDto::LogProgress` gains an optional `hlc` (serde default, omitted when empty; old queues and other clients keep working) = the client's stamp for the status the log implies, and `App::log_progress_stamped` uses it for the event's `StatusChange` (`App::stamp` still refuses stamps more than the allowed drift ahead, DEC-126); without it the server stamps it now, as before, so online logs and the DEC-126 convergence test are unchanged. An entry the log itself creates starts with the smallest stamp (DEC-128), so an old client stamp still applies there. The web queues a log with `Offline::stamp()` (next step, episode marks). Result: offline changes count as made when they were made; across devices the later change wins (LWW on the HLC, clock skew within the drift limit as for every sync client). Tests: `apps/server/tests/offline.rs` `a_queued_change_loses_to_a_later_change_from_another_device` (new: phone queue a minute old with a log, a rating and a status; laptop sets completed and 80 now; after the flush completed/80 stay and the log is kept) and `a_queue_replayed_twice_changes_nothing` (adapted to one push with queue stamps; it failed with `in_progress` instead of `paused` while the server still ignored the log's stamp, then passed); web unit test `commands_keep_the_op_id_and_carry_the_stamp` (one push in queue order with the queue's stamps; a log carries its stamp). Not run: the new device test against the DEC-434 code (it passed in the intermediate state where the client already sent queue stamps) | keep DEC-434 and document it (the user loses later edits made elsewhere); a server rule that a log never moves an explicitly stamped status (changes DEC-126 semantics) | restore `log_batch`/`field_batch` in `crates/web/src/offline.rs` and ignore `hlc` in `crates/api/src/sync.rs` | DEC-126, DEC-128, DEC-434
DEC-456 | WP4-8: UX3-25 panic guard and the "reactive value disposed" panic (DEC-384) | Guard: `cargo xtask e2e` starts the Playwright web server with `E2E_SERVER_LOG` (a file in the system temp dir, outside the repo; `playwright.config.ts` wraps the server command in `sh -c 'exec ... >>"$E2E_SERVER_LOG" 2>&1'` only when it is set), then scans it for `panicked at` (`xtask::e2e_log::panics`, unit tests `xtask/tests/e2e_log.rs`), prints each panic with its message and the line count, deletes the file and fails the run on any panic (after the Playwright result). Runs on 2026-11-08/09: run 1 117 passed, 0 panics; run 2 failed: 103 passed, 3 failed (History "Show more", the Automation channel's "Last delivery failed", the game group menu), 11 not run, and one panic `reactive_graph-0.2.16/src/traits.rs:328:43: At leptos-0.8.23/src/transition.rs:115:27, you tried to access a reactive value which was defined at transition.rs:104:21, but it has already been disposed` - UX3-25 reproduced. Root cause (from the sources): `transition.rs:104` is the Transition's task set, an `ArcRwSignal` that cannot be disposed; `with` panics with that message whenever `try_read` returns `None`, and `Plain::try_new` (`signal/guards.rs:106`) takes the read with guardian's non-blocking `try_take`, so a read while another thread holds the write lock (a resource's `TaskHandle` dropped on another tokio worker updates the set) panics. Line 115 is the "none pending" memo, which on the server is only read by the `set_pending` effect (the SSR stream waits with its own `try_read_untracked` effect, `suspense_component.rs:378`), and the only `set_pending` in the app is the Library results Transition. Fix: the server renders that Transition without `set_pending` (`#[cfg(feature = "ssr")]`; the browser keeps it for `aria-busy`), the rows moved into `ResultRows`. After the fix three full runs: 117 passed each, 0 panics (237, 237, 233 log lines). The three failures of run 2 did not repeat and are taken as fallout of that run (one page aborted by the panic, the rest not proven); the `SignalSetter::map`/`try_set` approach was not tried again. Considered and not done: a patched copy of `leptos` through `[patch.crates-io]` with `try_with` in both memos (refused by the tool policy for vendoring third-party code, and broader than needed); a single-threaded SSR executor (throughput). Other Transitions or Suspense boundaries are not affected on the server (they never read the memo there); if a later `set_pending` is added it must be browser-only too | keep the guard only | remove the cfg pair in `crates/web/src/library.rs` `Results`; drop `E2E_SERVER_LOG` in `xtask/src/main.rs` and `playwright.config.ts` | DEC-384; leptos 0.8.23 `transition.rs`, `suspense_component.rs`; reactive_graph 0.2.16 `traits.rs`, `signal/guards.rs`; guardian 1.3.0 `try_take` docs ("This function does not block"); e2e runs 2026-11-08/09

DEC-460 | UX4-1: a dirty mark of the statistics cache could be lost (a writer's trigger hit a committed mark with ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING, took no lock, and committed after the reader's `DELETE ... RETURNING` and recompute) | Fixed with a generation per mark (migration `0029_stats_dirty_gen.sql`): every marking trigger now does `ON CONFLICT (user_id, item_id) DO UPDATE SET gen = EXCLUDED.gen` (a new value from the sequence `stats_dirty_gen`, so the writer holds the row lock until it commits); a refresh reads `(item_id, gen)` without taking the marks, recomputes, and at the end deletes only the marks that still carry the generation it read and that no open writer holds (`FOR UPDATE ... SKIP LOCKED`, `stats_cache.rs::take_marks`). A mark a writer touched during the refresh survives; the reader never waits for a writer and a writer waits for a reader only between its final delete and commit (before, a writer's DO NOTHING waited while a refresh was deleting the row, up to a full rebuild). `stats_zone` now runs on the refresh's own connection (UX4-22). Test-first: `store-pg/tests/stats_race.rs` `a_mark_set_by_an_uncommitted_writer_survives_a_concurrent_refresh` (two connections: writer open, refresh, commit, compare with `month_amounts_full`) and `interleaved_writers_and_readers_end_with_the_full_statement` (3 writers x 15 transactions held open 0-14 ms, 2 readers x 25 reads) both failed on the old code (stale by one event), pass now | the review's DO UPDATE with the DELETE at the start (fixes the loss, but every writer then waits for a running refresh); SERIALIZABLE for the refresh (retries) | revert 0029 in a new migration and restore `DELETE ... RETURNING` | review UX4-1; PostgreSQL 18 docs "INSERT ... ON CONFLICT", "SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED", "Read Committed isolation"
DEC-461 | UX4-2: fair share restarted rounds at 0 for a user with nothing queued, so a steady stream of single requests from others starved a queued burst | Fixed in `catalog_core::gate`: the gate keeps the round of the interactive waiter served last (`current_round`) and each user's last round given (`last_round`, pruned below the current round); a new fair waiter gets `max(own last + 1, current_round)`, also when it takes a free permit at once. So a user who was just served queues behind the rounds in progress and users take turns. Test-first: `review_tests::a_steady_stream_of_other_users_does_not_starve_a_queued_burst` (A queues 5, B and C ask 10 times each one at a time; A's fifth was served 25th of 25, now before position 15) failed, passes; DEC-402's `hosted_mode_serves_interactive_waiters_round_robin_per_user` still passes | the reviewer's `max(own + 1, current)` without remembering served rounds (still starves when two other users alternate: each re-asks at the current round) | restore the per-waiter scan in `acquire_for` | review UX4-2; design.md 4.1
