DEC-464 | UX4-5: undoing any bulk op rewrote status, rating, notes and the whole tag set with fresh stamps, so a change made in between was reverted | Fixed: `app::BulkUndo` is now the inverse of the one op (an enum: `Status` with the stamp the op wrote per item, `TagAdded`, `TagRemoved` with the spelling each entry had, `ListGained`, `Restore` with the removed entries, `Nothing`), in `app/src/bulk/undo.rs`. A status goes back only while it still carries the op's stamp (a newer change wins); a tag comes off or back only on the items the op touched, other tags, status, rating and notes keep their values and stamps; a removed entry comes back only when the item was not added again meanwhile; a list loses only what it gained. Tests: `app/tests/bulk_undo.rs` (change in between then undo, per op) failed on the old code (5 of 5), pass; `app/tests/bulk.rs` kept passing (one assertion switched from `encode/decode` to the JSON round trip). Remaining window: between the undo's read and its write a concurrent write to the same entry can still be overwritten (read-then-write, as every bulk op) | refuse the undo when any `*_hlc` is newer than the snapshot (less useful: one unrelated change blocks it); per-field LWW in the upsert SQL | restore the snapshot-based `undo_bulk` | review UX4-5; DEC-417
DEC-465 | UX4-12: unsigned client-held undo token, unvalidated fields, a token larger than the body limit, the list half not atomic, 500 single reads | Fixed: the token is `v1.<base64 json>.<base64 HMAC-SHA256>` (the pending-login cookie format, `auth::oidc::{seal_signed, open_signed}`) with a key derived by HKDF from the live key ring under its own purpose (`UNDO_KEY_INFO = "bulk-undo-token"`; every loaded key verifies, so a rotation does not break a pending undo; without the admin area, small test setups, a random per-process key), and carries the user id and an expiry of one hour (`UNDO_TTL_SECS`); an edited, foreign or expired token answers 400 `bad_request`. A bulk op whose undo would exceed `app::MAX_UNDO_BYTES` (1 MiB of JSON; base64 and the wrapper stay under the 2 MiB `JSON_BODY_LIMIT`) is refused before anything changes (422 `invalid`, field `item_ids`); a restored entry is never added later than now; the list undo writes no entries (atomic by construction) and a list deleted since is nothing to undo (204); an op that added nothing gives `Nothing`; entry ids are checked with one read (`LibraryRepo::existing_entry_ids`, default per id, one `= ANY` on Postgres). Tests: `api::bulk::tests` (signature, user, expiry, wrong key, edited body; the largest undo fits the body limit) and `apps/server/tests/library_page.rs` `an_undo_token_is_signed_and_bound_to_its_user` (edited and someone else's token: 400, nothing changed) failed against an unsigned stub first, pass; `app/tests/bulk_undo.rs` covers the size refusal and the deleted list | server-side snapshots keyed by id and user with an expiry (DEC-417's alternative; a table and a sweep); blake3 keyed hash | return to the unsigned JSON | review UX4-12; RFC 2104; RFC 5869
DEC-466 | UX4-9 (orchestrator decision, the owner may reverse it): TMDB terms 1.C and the title and year DEC-453 kept; unowned items refreshed forever | Strict reading of 1.C ("any information obtained through or from TMDB"): at the limit without a possible refresh the purge now also clears title (to '') and year, so only the external id and the person's own data (status, history, ratings, notes, tags, lists) stay; HTTP cache bodies older than the limit go as before. `CatalogItem::title_unavailable()` (empty title) tells clients to show a neutral placeholder; server-rendered notification texts already do ("Title unavailable - source data expired", notify `item_unavailable-title` en/de); the web pages are the web workstream's (see its notes). A purged item is fetched again automatically once TMDB is usable: `catalog_items.provider_refetch_after` (migration `0030_catalog_expiry.sql`) is set at the purge, the hourly sweep queues a `catalog.refresh` for purged items whose time has come and moves it a day on (`REFETCH_EVERY_HOURS`), and a write that brings a title back clears it and stamps `provider_data_at` again. An item no library entry, list, session, progress event or review refers to is deleted at the limit instead of refreshed (`CatalogRepo::delete_unreferenced`, one guarded `DELETE` with an outbox `delete`; a reference inserted concurrently fails the NO ACTION foreign key and the item is kept). A purge also queues the library search documents of the item (they carry the title). Search-result fills no longer give a purged item a cover without a stamp. Tests (fake clock): `catalog-core/tests/retention.rs` `an_item_nobody_references_is_deleted_at_the_limit_instead_of_refreshed`, `a_purge_keeps_the_persons_own_data_and_refetches_once_tmdb_is_usable` (+3 updated: owned items, title and year gone), `store-pg/tests/retention.rs` `unreferenced_items_are_deleted_and_purged_ones_are_refetched_on_postgres` (+1 updated), `apps/server/tests/retention.rs` (job purges the owned item, deletes the unowned one), notify `an_expired_title_reads_as_the_placeholder`; the catalog-core tests first failed to compile (new report fields), the notify test failed on an assertion | keep title and year as the person's record (DEC-453; reverse by restoring `title`/`year` in both `purge_provider_data` and dropping the refetch); copy title and year into the library entry at purge time | see the alternatives; HANDOFF section 6 lists it for the owner | review UX4-9; TMDB API terms of use 1.C (as quoted in docs/connectors/tmdb.md, read 2026-11-08)

DEC-477 | Why did `item.spec.ts` "pages: +10 and a typed page log, a bad page says why, Finished completes" lose "Enter a page number from 1 on." (line 208, last full e2e run)? | Root cause: the inline page field (`PagesInline`, like `PositionInline`) kept its typed text and its error line in signals of its own render, and the item page renders the action bar afresh on every refetch (`ItemPage`'s keyed `<For>`, version per refetch). Logging page 120 refetches the page twice (the 300 ms debounced refresh after the log, then once more for its `entity.updated` event once the first window closed), so a refetch that landed after "soon" was typed either re-created the field with the stored "120" before Enter (Enter then logged 120 again) or dropped the error line right after Enter; the result depended on timing (same shape as DEC-288). Fix: `log_sheet::InlineDraft` (typed text while it differs from the stored value, and the error line) is owned by the item page (`ItemUi.inline`), cleared when another item opens and when a log went through; both inline fields take their value and error from it. The e2e test is the test (it failed in the last full run and is unchanged) | `settings::kept`-style slots (the item page has no `loaded`); stop rendering the action bar afresh (DEC-398 explains why rows are rendered afresh) | give the fields their own signals again | e2e run of 2026-11-09; DEC-288, DEC-398
DEC-478 | UX4-6: sign-out left the offline queue in `localStorage`, unsent and unannounced | `web::sign_out`: the account menu and the You hub submit one hidden form mounted by the shell (still a plain POST before hydration). Hydrated, the submit handler does nothing extra while the queue is empty; otherwise, online, it awaits one flush (`Offline::send`) and signs out when nothing is left; when changes still wait (offline, the push failed, a flush already running) a dialog says "One change is not synced yet" / "N changes ..." with "Stay signed in" and "Sign out and discard" (`Offline::discard` empties the record, which removes the key). Every sign-out through it forgets the recent items (UX4-22). So the per-user key `app-offline-<username>` is removed after a successful flush or an explicit discard and is never left for the next person. Tests: `sign_out::tests::sign_out_asks_only_while_changes_wait`, e2e `offline.spec.ts` "sign-out sends waiting changes first ..." (push blocked: the question, Stay keeps the change; push allowed: sent, signed out, no keys left) and "sign-out can discard ..." (discarded, nothing stored, the server never got it); the unit test was written with the code, not run first | `beforeunload` warning (not shown for a form post in every browser); flush and sign out regardless (loses the changes silently when the push fails) | restore the two plain forms | review UX4-6; design.md 5.4; DEC-432

Test: e2e `library.spec.ts` "the bulk bar at 360 px": one remove gives exactly one bulk event, `logged` | clear in `on_done` (also runs after errors and undos) | clear the selection before `run_bulk` again | review UX4-7; DEC-435

DEC-485 | Library select-all right after the page loads selected nothing while the box showed ticked (full e2e run of 2026-11-09; the 360 px test in `library.spec.ts` then wrapped the step in a `toPass` retry) | Root cause not proven. What was ruled out, from the sources: hydration is synchronous (leptos_router 0.8.17 `FlatRoutesView::hydrate` hydrates the route with `now_or_never`, leptos 0.8.23 Suspense/Transition hydrate in a `RenderEffect` that runs at once, resources start from the serialized value, leptos_server 0.8.8 `resource.rs:303`), so the `body[data-hydrated]` marker (an effect of `App`) comes after the list's handlers exist; no code clears the selection on a click (the two clearing effects follow the URL state and the rows only); a refetch keeps the resource's previous value (reactive_graph 0.2.16 `arc_async_derived.rs:365-389`) and the results `<For>` keeps its key. Probes with debug logging (removed) under CPU throttling (x8, x10, x20), theme switches, a stream of instance-wide `entity.updated` events and 4 parallel workers did not reproduce it; the failure snapshot showed a live table, the header box ticked, no row ticked and no bar, i.e. the change handler did not select anything. Hardening (the step is the plain `check()` again, the `toPass` workaround is removed): the header and row checkboxes are rendered `disabled` until an effect of the list view has run in the browser (`library::live`; the server's HTML has them disabled, Playwright's `check` waits for enabled), select-all takes the rows on the page at the click, and after a change the header box shows the selection (`shows_selection` sets `checked`/`indeterminate` from it), so a click that selects nothing can no longer leave a ticked box (a `check()` would then fail as "did not change its state" instead of passing silently). Results: the 360 px test passed 5/5 alone without the retry before the hardening and 5/5 in the `--repeat-each 5` run of library/home/keyboard after it; full suite afterwards: 124 passed, 0 panics in the server log (`cargo xtask e2e-scan`: 242 lines) | find the race first (not reproduced in about 30 targeted runs); keep the retry (hides a possible app bug) | remove `live`/`shows_selection`, put the `toPass` back | orchestrator item 1; corrects DEC-482 (its 360 px step)
DEC-486 | Offline, Home's next step was detached and Home showed "Home could not be loaded" (the 360 px banner test, rewritten to avoid it); also why Home re-rendered on every refetch | Root cause, reproduced: catalog changes are published to every signed-in page (`AppEvent::entity_updated("catalog_item", .., vec![])`, `app/src/catalog.rs`), and Home refetched on each `entity.updated` and rendered every answer afresh (`fresh()` key per refetch). In Playwright's offline mode the open event stream keeps delivering (a half-open connection does the same in a real browser), so each event refetched Home, the fetch failed (`net::ERR_INTERNET_DISCONNECTED`, a `_fn/home` request per event in the probe log) and Home turned into its error state under the pointer; it was not a loop in Home but other tests' imports. Fix: `offline::refresh_when_online` holds an event's refresh while the browser is offline and runs it once when it is back (Home, Library, History use it); Home's `Shown` keeps the render of an answer equal to the last one (same key), renders afresh only for a new answer (handlers carry the new data, UX1-2), and keeps the last answer when a refetch never reached the server (`offline::network_error`). Tests: e2e `offline.spec.ts` "offline, a change elsewhere on the instance leaves Home as it is" (the browser's `offline` event, another account adds a catalog item, the page's stream delivers it: no `_fn/home` request while offline, one after `online`, then a `_fn/home` aborted as `internetdisconnected` keeps Home and the next step logs), and the banner test is back to its first approach (a reload per theme, offline, click the next step, axe, reflow); helpers `changeElsewhere`, `countEntityEvents`. The first versions of the new test and of the banner test (both with `context.setOffline` and an awaited event from another account) failed before the fix ("Home could not be loaded"; click timeout) and passed after; the final version uses the `offline` event because Playwright's offline mode does not always keep the stream (one of five runs saw no event); unit test `home::tests::a_refetch_renders_afresh_only_for_a_new_answer_and_offline_keeps_the_last_one` (written with the code, not run red). `offline.spec.ts` passed 40/40 with `--repeat-each 5` | stop sending catalog events to everyone (server, larger change); key by the answer's JSON (DEC-385 style, more work per refetch) | revert `Shown` to `fresh(renders)` and the three event effects to `refresh.run` | orchestrator item 2; corrects DEC-483 (the banner test avoided this)

(checked by hand on a missing and an empty file). Locally this also catches `reuseExistingServer` picking up a server that was started without the log. DEC-490 | Catalog changes were published to every signed-in user (`entity_updated("catalog_item", .., vec![])` in `app/src/catalog.rs` and `episodes.rs`), so any import or refresh anywhere refetched every open Home/Library/History/item page of every user (DEC-486, DEC-488: focus loss, the History "Show more" reset, needless load). Who should hear about a catalog change? | `App::publish_item` asks `CatalogRepo::audience(item, now)` (one statement on Postgres, migration 0031) and publishes to: the users with the item in their library, history (progress events), reviews or on one of their lists, the owners of sessions of it and, while the owner shares sessions (G2-9), the users linked to its participants, and the users who opened its item page in the last hour (`catalog_watchers`, written by `ops::item_page` through `App::watch_item` before the refresh is queued, so a stub opened from search still fills in; a user's ended watches are dropped with their next one, rows go with the item or the user). An item on a list visible to the whole instance goes to everyone (empty audience, as before); an item nobody refers to is published to nobody, and a newly inserted item is not published at all (nobody can have it yet); a failing audience query publishes nothing (best effort, like every event). Migration 0031 also adds the missing `item_id` indexes on `library_entries`, `list_items` and `sessions` (they serve the retention delete too). The other `vec![]` audiences are admin topics (filtered to admins) and a test; nothing else was instance-wide. Tests (written first, seen failing against a stub that kept the old behaviour): `app/tests/catalog_audience.rs` (Alice's import and its refresh reach Alice only, Bob once he has the item; nobody's item: no event; list, session with a linked participant and the share switch, instance list; an item page watch and its end) and `store-pg/tests/catalog_audience.rs` (the same rules and the watch upkeep on Postgres); `app/tests/episodes.rs` now has a user own the show. e2e: `offline.spec.ts` checks that another account's import names nothing in the page's events (`entityIds`), and the specs that used another account's import as a background refresh (`offline.spec.ts`, keyboard "Home: J and K") now use the user's own change (`changeMine`: a new list, which Home does not show) | a per-page subscription of item ids over the event stream (more protocol, and the web and worker processes would have to share it); filter in the browser (`mentions`, the DEC-488 alternative: every page still gets every event); keep the broadcast and only harden the pages (DEC-486/488 did, the load stays) | publish `entity_updated("catalog_item", id, vec![])` again in `publish_item`, drop `ops::item_page`'s watch and migration 0031's table | orchestrator item (ux4 tag); DEC-486, DEC-488; sqlx 0.8 `query!` array output (`ARRAY(...) AS "users!: Vec<uuid::Uuid>"`)
DEC-491 | A refresh of History (an event or an undo) reset the pages loaded by "Show more" (`More::default()`) and refetched only the first page, and the stream rendered every new answer afresh, dropping the focus on "Show more" (home.spec "history keeps its filters", DEC-456 run 2, DEC-488) | The refresh asks the first-page resource for as many rows as are on screen for that address (`want`, taken by the next fetch; `feed_range` follows `next` in pages of up to 100, the feed's maximum), so one answer brings the loaded range back. "Show more" pages carry the first answer's `next` they continue (`More::after`); `shown_rows` appends them only to that answer, so a refetched answer (its own `next`) replaces them without a moment of missing or doubled rows. The stream's key no longer holds the rows of a non-empty answer (`stream_key`: address, stale, "rows"; empty and error answers still render afresh), `Days` follows the latest answer through a `Memo` in the browser (`latest_page`, the DEC-398 pattern: on the server the first answer only), and day groups are keyed by day and their rows' JSON, so only a day whose rows change renders again (its handlers carry the new data, UX1-2) and the "Show more" button stays mounted with its focus. Tests: e2e `home.spec.ts` "history keeps the pages 'Show more' loaded and the focus when an event refreshes it" (60 rows after one "Show more", a tag on the user's own item: still 60 and focused; a new episode today: 3 rows today, still 60, focused; "Show more" goes on to 66) failed before the change (30 rows after the event) and passes; unit tests `show_more_pages_belong_to_the_answer_they_continue` (new) and `the_stream_key_follows_the_answer_and_its_address` (now: same key for other rows, another for no rows) were written before the code (compile failure first) | merge the refetched first page into the loaded pages (rows have no stable id, a new row shifts every page); refetch each loaded page by its cursor (cursors move when rows are added); keep resetting and only keep the focus (loses what the user paged to) | revert `history.rs` `More`/`shown_rows`/`feed_range`/`want`/`latest_page`/`day_key` and the stream key | orchestrator item (ux4 tag); DEC-385, DEC-398, DEC-488
DEC-492 | DEC-490 regression: `latency.rs` `pages_never_wait_on_providers` waited in vain for `entity.updated` after `GET /items/{id}` of a stub nobody has (the refresh was queued, but only `ops::item_page` registered a watcher, so the audience was empty and nothing was published). Which requests that queue a refresh on behalf of a user must make that user hear about it? | `App::item_detail_for(actor, id)` (used by `GET /items/{id}`, `ops::item` now takes the actor): when the item needs a refresh, the actor watches it (`watch_item`, `ITEM_WATCH_SECS`) before the job is queued, so the refresh, the cover set after it and the episode dates that follow reach them; a fresh item writes nothing (no watch, no job). `App::item_detail` stays for the item page, which already watches unconditionally. Checked the other paths: search does not queue refreshes (connector results are inserted by `ensure_catalog_item` without an event, nobody can have them yet) and writes nothing; the palette's choices go through the item page (watched), `POST /lists/{id}/items` (the owner is in the audience), `POST /imports/{id}/match` (the replayed row puts the item in the user's library/history), the quick path (item page, then library add); the importer and the webhook integration queue the refresh right before the row's entry or play is written (a refresh that finished in between would miss the user; not seen, not fixed); the episode job is queued by the refresh and published to the same audience; the retention refetch has no user. Tests first in `app/tests/catalog_audience.rs`: `a_detail_request_that_queues_a_refresh_reaches_the_requester` and `episodes_fetched_after_a_detail_request_reach_the_requester` failed against a stand-in `item_detail_for` that kept the old behaviour (and before that did not compile), pass now; `a_detail_request_of_a_fresh_item_writes_no_watch` and `a_search_result_added_to_a_list_or_matched_by_an_import_reaches_its_user` pin the other paths (passed before too). `latency.rs` passes unchanged (entity.updated after 10.15 s) | watch on every `GET /items/{id}` (a write per read); watch the hits of every search (writes on the search path for items nobody opens) | call `item_detail` in `ops::item` again and drop `item_detail_for` | orchestrator item (ux4 tag); DEC-490
DEC-493 | Search p95 over budget in the release load run with 8 clients + 2 writers (301.6 ms in the orchestrator's run; 216/208 in DEC-476). What does a search cost per request, and what is cut? | Measured on a leftover load database (50 551 catalog docs, Postgres fallback backend, which the load test uses; the Meilisearch client is created once per process and reused): the statement took 55-125 ms for the five load queries, almost all in the `body % $3` trigram condition (36 000 index candidates rechecked for "juniper delta 777"); without it 10 ms. `search::pg` now ranks words before typos, like Meilisearch's ranking rules (words, then typo): first the documents holding the words (`tsv @@`) or the text (`ILIKE`), ordered by `ts_rank + similarity` as before; only when they do not fill the page are the closest misspellings added, by `word_similarity` (`$3 <% body`, default threshold 0.6) instead of whole-string `similarity`, so a typo in a long title matches too ("harry poter" finds "Harry Potter and the Philosopher's Stone": similarity 0.28, below the old 0.3 threshold; word_similarity 0.79). The typo part sits behind a one-time filter (`(SELECT count(*) FROM exact) < limit`) and is not executed when the page is full. psql timings for the load queries: 11-31 ms (was 55-125). `ops::search` reads the hits' items in one `get_many` (was one read per hit, up to 20) in hit order, and reads the member's credentials and spawns the fan-out only when a connector will be asked (`user_keys` returns early when no registered source takes a member credential). Nothing is written on the search path. Test first: `search/tests/suite.rs` "a typo in a long title" failed on the Postgres fallback before (Meilisearch passed), passes on both. Release runs, `LOAD_EVENTS=40000 LOAD_SESSIONS=20000 LOAD_SHARED=2000 LOAD_WRITERS=2 cargo xtask load --release --clients 8`, p95 ms: before (HEAD 0d39774 from a `git archive`, one run): search 282.7, History person 335.8 (the assert failed on History person), year 80.8, Home 179.9, query first/last 236.1/185.8, library first/last 40.7/47.0, activity 75.7, totals 111.1, series 75.4, POST 51.8, writers 63.6 (n 348), zone change 747. After, three runs on the working tree: run 1 (the first right after the 23-minute release build, as the before run was): search 122.4, History person 347.2 (failed on History person again), year 100.0, Home 183.3, query 225.7/175.9, library 41.7/41.2, activity 77.0, totals 117.2, series 100.9, POST 57.9, writers 72.9 (n 264), zone change 765; run 2: search 85.7, person 227.4, year 57.7, Home 183.5, query 215.4/144.4, library 33.8/38.7, activity 53.1, totals 67.4, series 36.7, POST 35.7, writers 47.8 (n 235), zone change 771, passed; run 3: search 84.0, person 232.3, year 58.0, Home 183.5, query 210.4/143.4, library 30.9/30.3, activity 45.7, totals 54.6, series 52.7, POST 33.1, writers 44.5 (n 236), zone change 775, passed. Search p50 181.7 -> 77.8/47.3/50.4. History person over 300 in both runs made right after a build (before and after alike, this change does not touch it) and about 230 in the two later runs; taken as host state after the build (it read about 200 in DEC-476), not proven | keep `similarity` and raise `pg_trgm.similarity_threshold` (a session setting, the pool is shared; 0.5 took 12 ms but drops more typos); a GiST index with KNN ordering (`<->`, about 30 ms here plus a second index); typo-only fallback in Rust as a second round trip | restore the single statement in `search/src/pg.rs` (`body % $3` in the condition) and the per-hit read in `ops::search` | orchestrator item (ux4 tag); DEC-399, DEC-476; PostgreSQL 17 docs F.35 pg_trgm (`similarity`, `word_similarity`, `<%`, `pg_trgm.word_similarity_threshold` default 0.6, GIN index support) and 14.1 EXPLAIN (One-Time Filter); Meilisearch docs "Ranking rules" (words, typo, ...)
DEC-494 | History "person" was the slowest endpoint of the release load run (8 clients + 2 writers, 40k events, 20k sessions, 2k shared): p95 335.8 / 347.2 ms in the runs right after a build (the 300 ms assert failed), about 230 later (DEC-493). Why, and what reads the person filter now? | Cause (EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS) on a load database from the debug harness, 40k events, 22 000 sessions, 50 551 items): the person filter ran the generic window walk (DEC-401/469) and dropped rows afterwards; Anna is on every tenth play (one row per 20 h), so 21 rows took four doubling windows (41, 82, 164, 328 rows per source), about 48 statements per request (counted from the code): per window events (328 rows: 2.8 ms, 1 341 buffers), own sessions (0.4 ms), shared sessions (6.4 ms each: no time index on `sessions` without the owner, so a seq scan of all 22 000 sessions hashed against the companion's 2 000 participations, then a top-N sort; 328 shared rows reach back 6 560 h, almost all outside the window), the participants of every session read (1.4 ms per 328), four pairing reads, names and up to 328 catalog items; about 1 200 sessions and 600 events read for 20 rows (estimated from the seed spacing). Fix: `ActivityQuery.person` now drives the reads from the person's sessions (`app/src/activity/person.rs`): a round reads the next "anchors" (sessions with the person or anyone merged into them, `with_participants_window`, `limit + 1` doubling to 2 000), turns each into a region of 121 s around it (every row with the person lies there: a "watched with" event is under 121 s from its session, a play's event is written at the session's start, a standalone or shared row is at it), merges regions, and builds every region's rows exactly as the window walk builds a window's (same pairing reads two and three spans up and one down, from memory), from one read per source for the whole round: new `ProgressRepo::recent_spans`, `SessionRepo::by_owner_spans` and `with_participants_spans` (`HistorySpans`: disjoint `[from, to)` spans, kinds, limit; trait defaults read one window per span, Postgres reads all spans in one statement); the shared read runs only when an anchor has another owner (only then can a shared row carry the person). Time between regions is skipped. A round whose span reads come back full (8 rows per anchor) hands over to the window walk at the point reached, so dense data stays correct. The Postgres span statements materialise the span rows first (`WITH ... AS MATERIALIZED`): with the `LIMIT` outside, the planner walked all 60 011 index entries of the user against the spans (344 ms, 228 226 buffers); now 0.5 ms (one `progress_events_user_recent` range scan per span); the kind is read per row by key (a join or `EXISTS` inside the `OR` hashed all 50 551 items, 14-21 ms; now 0.5 ms with a kind filter). A first page is now about 11 statements (counted from the code): user, companions, representing, linked, anchors 21 (6.2 ms, still a seq scan of sessions, see alternatives) plus participants, events in 21 spans (0.5 ms, 28 rows), own sessions (0.15 ms, 21 rows) plus participants, names, items. Same rows as before: the full-read caveat of DEC-469 (a chain of three or more events and sessions of one item within two minutes of each other across a boundary) now applies at region ends too. Tests: `app/tests/activity_person.rs` `person_pages_equal_the_full_read_filtered_by_person` (pages of 1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 20, 100 equal the unfiltered full read filtered by the person's participations: a merged person, a lone duplicate, a person without merges, a shared person of an owner who shares and of one who does not, year, type and kind filters, shared only; "watched with" 100 s away, undone plays, a burst of 70 events that makes small pages hand over to the window walk); it passed on the old code too (person walk switched off), so the semantics are unchanged; it fails with a region of 90 s and fails without the dense hand-over. `store-pg/tests/windows.rs`: `span_reads_return_the_full_reads_rows_in_the_spans_on_postgres` (the three span reads equal the full reads in the spans, with and without kinds, cut at a limit) and `the_person_feed_matches_the_full_read_on_postgres` (pages of 1, 2, 3, 20). DEC-499 | Palette: (a) `ui::Command` filtered the caller's items again by substring, so a typo-tolerant server hit never showed; (b) a `search.results` event that arrived before the HTTP answer of its search was dropped, because the palette learned the search id only from the answer. How are both fixed without an API change? | (a) `Command` gets a prop `filter: Option<Signal<bool>>` (default: filter as typed, so the gallery and other callers are unchanged); the palette passes `false` while it shows search results and `true` for the idle list (recent items, commands). (b) `palette.rs` `Stream`: while a search is in flight, events with an unknown id are held (at most 32) and the answer takes the ones of its own search in arrival order; a newer search, a failed search or a cleared field drops what was held; events of the search on screen are applied at once as before | (b) let the browser make the search id and send it with the search (needs a parameter on the server function, the backend trait, the REST query and the OpenAPI document; the diagnosis calls it "a separate small change"); subscribe to events before sending (does not help: the id is still unknown) | (a) remove the `filter` prop and its use in `palette.rs`; (b) replace `Stream` by the plain `search_id` signal again | fix pack brief F5 item 5; `docs/fable/round2/bug-diagnosis.md` (OT-1, "Not a cause but found on the way"); tests `ui/tests/wasm.rs` command_shows_items_as_given_when_filtering_is_off, `palette::tests::a_batch_that_beats_the_answer_waits_for_it`, `shell.spec.ts` "palette: a typo still lists what the server found"

Candidate 4 (a list moving under a resting pointer takes the row) did not show in Chromium, before or after the fix, so the `mousemove` handler is unchanged. Changes in `ui::Command`: the active option is remembered by item id (a new query starts at the first row; when the item leaves the list the first row is active); the active row gets `outline-2 -outline-offset-2 outline-ring` on top of `bg-accent` (token `--ring`; the test computes at least 3:1 against the row and against the list background in the light and the dark theme, WCAG 1.4.11); ArrowUp/ArrowDown call `scrollIntoView` with `block: nearest` on the option, once at once and once on the next animation frame (the key hints make the active row higher), and the first row also scrolls the list to its top so its group heading shows. Tab still does not reach the options (APG combobox pattern, as the diagnosis records). For the test the TMDB mock answers a show search that starts with "fight club" 1.5 s late with one synthetic show | a start border instead of an outline (shifts the text); a ring via box-shadow (harder to assert, same tokens); index-based active state kept and re-pointed after each batch (the id is the simpler truth); a pointer-movement check on `mousemove` (not proven needed; add it if Firefox or Safari show candidate 4) | restore `crates/ui/src/command.rs` and the two web-sys features in `crates/ui/Cargo.toml`; drop the tests and the `lateShow` block of `src/web/tests/tmdb-mock.mjs` | fix pack brief F5 item 6; `docs/fable/round2/bug-diagnosis.md` OT-1; tests `shell.spec.ts` "palette: arrow keys move through provider results; ...", `ui/tests/wasm.rs` command_keeps_the_active_option_when_items_are_appended and command_scrolls_the_active_option_into_view_and_marks_it; web-sys 0.3.107 `Element::scroll_into_view_with_scroll_into_view_options` (compiled and run in the wasm tests)

The line goes away with the next mark that logs something and when another season is chosen. No bulk rewatch, no question "log them again?" (waits for the owner's choice between outcome A and B of the diagnosis) | disable the buttons and menu items on fully watched ranges (a disabled control does not say why, and "Mark up to here" would need the state per row); a toast (not tied to the place, gone after a few seconds) | remove `Bulk` / `plan_bulk` and the `note` signal; call `unwatched_*` in `episode_list.rs` as before; drop the three `episodes-nothing-*` strings | fix pack brief F5 item 7; `docs/fable/round2/bug-diagnosis.md` OT-15 ("Minimum fix that needs no decision"); tests `episode_plan::tests::a_bulk_mark_with_nothing_open_says_so`, `item.spec.ts` "a bulk mark with nothing left to mark says so"
DEC-502 | OT-11 read side: a play is stored with scores, the winner, the co-op outcome and the duration, and no row showed them ("Session logged" only; the item page's History tab had no people at all). Display: `web::history::play_line` adds one line to the shared activity row (History, Home, item History tab): who won ("Ben won", "You won", several names for a shared win) or the co-op outcome ("Won together" / "Lost together"), the scores ("Scores: You 31, Ben 42.5"; alone "Score: 12"), the duration ("45 min"), joined by " - "; en and de. The item History tab also shows "with ..." for plays now. Left as it is: the head text "Session logged" (copy is with the owner's UX review), the Plays tab of design.md 6.3, wins and hours on Stats (OT-17), people on the item History rows of "watched with" sessions of other kinds (they are paired by time in the feed, not by an event id) | one statement for all sessions of the item (corrected by DEC-506: this needs a port method only, the statement `sessions_where_ids` existed; done there); let the browser call `GET /sessions` and filter (reads every session of the owner on each item page); an `item` filter on the activity feed (the feed pages by time windows, an item filter would walk them all); put duration and outcome into the head sentence as the UX pass proposes ("Played 1 h 35 min with Anna and Jonas. DEC-503 | Review OT1A-1: since DEC-499 the palette shows server results unfiltered; the rows of the previous query stayed on screen, first row active, until the next answer (250 ms debounce plus the round trip) and for good when that search failed, so Enter or Ctrl+Enter took a row the field no longer named. DEC-510 | Review OT1A-11: while a search is in flight `palette::Stream` holds every `search.results` event with another id, at most 32; more than 32 foreign events inside one round trip (two tabs of one account searching, many sources) would drop an early batch of the current search. Change the eviction? | Deferred, no code change. Both proposed fixes only move which event is lost: dropping the oldest loses exactly the early batch of the current search when the foreign events come after it, and one slot per `(search_id, connector)` is not a bound (every foreign search adds keys). The palette cannot tell the events apart before the answer names its id; the fix that removes the case is a search id made by the client and sent with the request (an API change, already the alternative recorded in DEC-499). Until then the loss needs more than 32 events of other searches within one request, with a handful of sources per search | drop the oldest; newest per search and connector; a larger `MAX_HELD` (same shape, later) | nothing to reverse | review OT1A-11; `crates/web/src/palette.rs` `Stream::arrived`; DEC-499

DEC-531 | Owner ruling HUB-0: is the hub built, and what happens to workstream W3 "board games on Wikidata"? | The hub is built as designed: H1 (hub role, read API, `hub` connector, Wikidata seed) and H2 (peer registration, signed requests) now, the reference hub open to the owner's own instances only, third parties after H5. H1 replaces W3: the rules of DEC-192 to DEC-198 (CC0 seed from Wikidata, etiquette, Commons images, per-value source marker) stay and are carried out by the hub role instead of an instance-level Wikidata connector; the owned flag and "Mark owned" (DEC-420) come with H1. Confirmed with it (defaults, owner-approved): CC0 1.0 for hub facts (HUB-1), instance-attributed contributions with an optional account (HUB-3), invite and approval at launch (HUB-9), the owner as first moderator (HUB-10), the full notice-and-action flow plus a lawyer (HUB-11), a second instance as staging hub (HUB-13), no Open Products Facts connector (HUB-15, closes the open question of HANDOFF 4.6), salted hashes of purged TMDB text for at most the retention limit with a lawyer question (HUB-6). HUB-12: monetisation is decided later, nothing is built for it | H1 only; no hub and W3 as an instance connector (DEC-192 as written) | stop after H1: the hub is one role of the same binary, off by default (`hub.serve = false`) | owner-answers-2026-11-10.md section 1; hub-design.md section 12 on branch wip/fable-reviews
DEC-532 | Owner rulings HUB-2, HUB-4 and WP-OD8: image licences and the scope of the hub | (1) HUB-2 b: uploaders choose CC BY 4.0 or CC0 only; the revocable operator licence ("model B") the owner had asked for is not built, its draft clause is shelved. The owner withdrew the wish on learning that Creative Commons licences cannot be revoked; the note also allows the review's suggestion (model B as an opt-in), so the point is asked back, and (b) is in force. The upload form must say that the licence cannot be withdrawn; "remove my name" and a courtesy take-down must exist. (2) HUB-4, free text: the hub is to cover every kind the product tracks ("a full feature hub. Including everything we do"), and may start without movies and shows. The kind table of hub-design.md section 11 ("not needed" for book, comic, podcast, music, visual novel; movie and show "not planned") becomes an order, not the scope. Limits that stay: the hub is CC0, so only CC0-compatible values and contributor-typed facts are stored, never TMDB, IGDB, MAL, BGG, VNDB, TVmaze or Wikipedia text; movies and shows open last and only after the lawyer has seen the provenance filter. DEC-533 | Owner ruling SEC-3 (free text): what do records that outlive an account keep about the person? | No name. "Audit rows don't need the name. It's fine to keep an identifier that is never stored anywhere else"; removing the person from the identifier list makes the rest untraceable; "It should be the normal flow for this globally". Rule for the product: records that outlive an account (audit log, job and import reports, notification log, hub change history) carry a random per-user subject id used nowhere else and never a name, e-mail address or provider subject, also for targets; names are resolved at read time; deleting the account deletes the subject mapping, the person's own content (notes, reviews, people entries, lists), their search documents, stored objects and invite addresses; client addresses in kept records are blanked at deletion; retention of kept records stays 365 days (OD-13); backups are covered by a stated retention and a replay list of deleted subject ids. Changes `admin_audit` (`migrations/0016_admin.sql:105-116`: `actor_name`, usernames in `detail`, `client_addr`) and replaces the two options of the audit's fix (pseudonymise on delete, or keep 365 days with names). The requested review, with the five conditions under which key deletion counts as erasure and the cases it does not cover, is in the answers file 11.3. Open with the owner: the display-name snapshot that `App::delete_user` writes into other members' people entries (`app/src/users.rs:113-125`) | keep names 365 days and say so in the privacy page (matches OD-13 literally); keep 30 days then pseudonymise; `actor_id` SET NULL only (loses "the same deleted person did these") | keep `actor_name` and state the retention in the privacy page | owner-answers-2026-11-10.md 11.3; security-audit.md SEC-3 on branch wip/fable-reviews

The private-range filter for OIDC endpoint hosts (discovery, token, JWKS, userinfo) and the mail host takes its default from the hosting mode; `notify.allow_private_targets` (`crates/config/src/lib.rs:381`) stays the explicit override in both directions. Built in every mode: no redirects on the governed HTTP client (or the allowlist re-checked per hop), categorised errors only for discovery and mail-test failures. The security audit's default (block everywhere unless the switch is on) is not taken, because the default hosting mode is home and a home identity provider or mail relay is usually on a private address (review page note SEC-4a) | a (block unless switched on, every mode); c (redirect fix and categorised errors only) | make the filter's default "on" in every mode | owner-answers-2026-11-10.md section 2 (SEC-4); security-audit.md SEC-4
DEC-535 | Owner ruling SEC-5 b: sign-in throttling | A budget per client address, a cap on concurrent password verifications and a progressive delay replace the hard lock of 10 failures in 15 minutes per name (`crates/auth/src/lib.rs:192-200`); counters in Postgres. This changes the behaviour behind OD-17 "keep the throttle" on the break-glass `/sign-in/local`: it is slowed, not locked, so nobody can keep it shut by guessing; the audit row and the admin notification stay. The owner asked "Does it make sense to do that much login logic if we use an idm anyway?": yes for the three behaviours (home installs have no identity provider, break-glass is the door used when the provider is down, every attempt costs an argon2 run), no for the Postgres counters while one web process is the supported shape (OPS-12 a, same page). Proposed and asked back: build the behaviours behind a counter interface with the in-memory store, add the Postgres store when several web processes are supported. DEC-565 | TBIN: the 25 files in `apps/server/tests/` each linked the whole server (15 executables of 522-524 MB, 10 of 100-128 MB, 8.36 GB together); a full test build no longer fits the 57 GB build host. How are they built? | One integration test target `it`: `apps/server/Cargo.toml` sets `autotests = false` and `[[test]] name = "it" path = "tests/it/main.rs"`; every former file is a module (`tests/it/<name>.rs`, declared in `main.rs`), `support/` is `crate::support` (`mod support;` became `use crate::support;`), test names are unchanged behind the module prefix (`item_page::a_watched_movie_offers_the_rewatch`). One module: `cargo test -p server --features gallery,all-connectors --test it -- item_page::` (several filters may follow); `cargo xtask load` runs `cargo test -p server --test it -- --ignored --nocapture load::`, so the second ignored test (`oidc::live_provider_honours_max_age_zero`) is not started. All tests now run as threads of one process (before: one process per file, files one after another). Checked for state that relied on a process per file: the tests set no environment variable or working directory and hold no statics, every listener binds port 0, temp directories carry a UUID, every test creates its own database (`store_pg::testing::fresh_db_url`); in the server, `page_routes()` (OnceLock, DEC-384) lists the routes once per process before any router exists, the undo key of `api::bulk` without the admin area is a process-random key used only to sign and verify, `telemetry::init` runs only in `main.rs`, `opendal::install_default` is idempotent. Nothing had to change and no test is serial. Measured 2026-11-11 (4 vCPU, debug): before 100 passed / 2 ignored in 25 binaries (106 passed with the 6 unit tests of the lib target); after 100 passed / 2 ignored in one binary of 552 MB (106 / 2 with the unit tests), twice through cargo, six more times directly and three times with `--test-threads 12`, 0 failed each time, about 21 s per run. `latency::pages_never_wait_on_providers` (one request under 200 ms) used to run alone in its process and passed all eleven runs next to the other tests; if it ever fails under load, measure the best of several requests instead of making it serial. Running the executable by hand needs `RUST_MIN_STACK=8388608` (DEC-188; cargo sets it from `.cargo/config.toml`), otherwise the first page render overflows the stack. Other crates: none with more than 5 integration test files links the server (`app` 19, `store-pg` 10, `auth` 6 files), left as they are | about 5 binaries by area (TEST-7 of the round 2 review: less saving, same rules for shared state); keep one binary per file and delete executables between batches (what the baseline run here had to do); a `tests/it.rs` with `#[path]` modules (files stay in place, but cargo would still discover each as a target without `autotests = false`) | move `tests/it/*.rs` and `tests/it/support/` back to `tests/`, restore `mod support;`, delete `main.rs`, the `autotests` line and the `[[test]]` table, and set `xtask::load::cargo_args` back to `--test load` | docs/handoff/fable-briefs/TBIN-one-test-binary.md; docs/fable/round2/tbin-progress.md; Cargo book "Cargo Targets" (target auto-discovery, `autotests`; `tests/<dir>/main.rs` is one target) https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/cargo-targets.html and "Package Layout" https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/guide/project-layout.html; rustc book "Tests" (several filters, `--ignored`, `--test-threads`) https://doc.rust-lang.org/rustc/tests/index.html
DEC-566 | ot2b, OT-10 (DEC-539): how the centered log dialog is built | A third presentation in `ui`, `FormDialog` (next to `Dialog` and `Sheet`, same `Panel`: role, label, focus in, Tab trap, Escape, `can_close`, return of focus). The panel is a column of three parts: the title, a body that scrolls (`[data-dialog-body]`, `min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-y-auto`) and a footer passed as a `ViewFn` (`[data-dialog-footer]`). The footer is a sibling of the scroller, not a sticky element inside it, so no field can be under it and no `scroll-padding` is needed (WCAG 2.2 SC 2.4.11). Below 640 px (`sm`) it is a bottom sheet over the full width, at most 85 dvh, with `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` under the footer; from 640 px it is centered, at most `100dvh - 2rem` high and `100% - 2rem` wide. `DialogWidth::Regular` is 32 rem (`sm:max-w-lg`, the watched dialog), `DialogWidth::Wide` 42 rem (`sm:max-w-2xl`, every play dialog, also a video game session whose scores are behind "More": one width per flow). The stretching: `Dialog` and `Sheet` keep their grid and get `content-start`, so a full-height side sheet no longer spreads its rows; the form dialog has no grid on the panel at all. `SheetSide::Adaptive` stays for the two short forms that still use it ("Add a person", "New list"); they were not moved (not log flows, DEC-539 keeps the Sheet). The component names `WatchedSheet` / `PlaySheet` and the file name `log_sheet.rs` are unchanged to keep the diff small | a prop `adaptive` on `Dialog` (every caller of `Dialog` would carry a footer slot it does not use); a sticky footer inside one scroller with `scroll-padding-bottom` (the design's wording; the footer height changes with the discard question and the errors, so a fixed padding would be wrong); move "Add a person" and "New list" too | use `Sheet side=SheetSide::Adaptive` in `log_sheet.rs` again and move the footer's content back into the forms; remove `content-start` from `GRID_PANEL` | owner-answers-2026-11-10.md OT-10; ux-owner-test.md 2.2; WCAG 2.2 "Focus Not Obscured (Minimum)" https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/#focus-not-obscured-minimum; Tailwind CSS v4 docs "max-width" (container scale: `lg` 32rem, `2xl` 42rem) https://tailwindcss.com/docs/max-width and "Responsive design" (`sm` 40rem) https://tailwindcss.com/docs/responsive-design; tests `ui/tests/render.rs` (form_dialog_*, grid_panels_do_not_stretch_their_rows), `ui/tests/wasm.rs` form_dialog_traps_focus_over_body_and_footer_and_returns_it, `gallery.spec.ts` "form dialog: ...", `item.spec.ts` "the log dialogs are centered ..."
DEC-567 | ot2b: what the footer of the log dialogs holds, and how Save reaches the form | The footer (`log_sheet::LogFooter`) holds, top to bottom: the discard question (D15, as before, focus on "Keep editing"), the errors of the save (a list, at most 6 rem high, then it scrolls), and the buttons "Cancel" (new, outline; `log-cancel`, en + de) and "Save". The question and the errors moved out of the form because the form scrolls now: at its end they could be out of view after Escape or a failed save; above the buttons they are always visible. The error next to the date field stays at the field. Cancel asks `can_close` like Escape and the close button, so a changed form gets the discard question. Save is outside the `<form>` and names it with the `form` attribute (`watched-form`, `play-form`); it is then the form's default button, so Enter in a text field still saves (HTML "implicit submission"). Below 640 px Save is 44 px high and as wide as the sheet (addendum-1 D10, ux-owner-test 2.2 "a full-width 44 px Save button") and Cancel is hidden there: the close button and Escape stay, and a second full-width button would cost a row of the sheet. OD-01 counts as before: the watched dialog opens with 2 inputs (when, people), a board game play with 5 for one player (when, duration, people, score, winner); the score rows of more players are read as one group (ux-owner-test 2.3; not owner-reviewed) | keep question and errors inside the form and scroll them into view; show Cancel on phones too | move the three parts back into `WatchedFields` / `PlayFields`; drop `max-sm:hidden` from Cancel | ux-owner-test.md 2.2, 2.3; addendum-1 D10, D15; HTML Standard, `form` attribute https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/form-control-infrastructure.html#attr-fae-form and implicit submission https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/form-control-infrastructure.html#implicit-submission; `item.spec.ts` "the log dialogs are centered ..."
DEC-568 | Review OT2A-8 (ot2a, deferred to ot2b by DEC-564): two adds or an add and a log overlap; the Undo of the add toast removed an entry that was changed meanwhile | (1) `AddBar`: while a first log or an add is on its way both buttons are disabled (the one that runs shows its spinner) and their handlers return early; "Log a play" waits for a running add too. (2) The Undo of "Added to your library as Planned" (`next_step::toast_added`: item page and palette) reads the item page first and removes the entry only while it is as the add left it (`item_words::add_undo` on `entry_untouched`: Planned, no event, rating, note or tag). A changed entry stays and the answer is the toast "Kept in your library: it has changed since you added it." (`item-added-kept`, en + de); an entry already removed by hand answers "Undone". This is the rule of DEC-561 for the other add toast. Not closed: the check and the removal are two requests, so a change between them (well under a second) can still be removed with the entry; and between the answer of the add and the refetched page the two buttons are enabled for a moment | a server use case "remove if untouched" (one request, no window; a new endpoint for one caller); disable Undo in the toast once the entry changes (the toast does not hear about the entry) | restore the old body of `toast_added`; drop `disabled=either` in `AddBar` | review docs/reviews/2026-11-10-ot2a-review.md OT2A-8; DEC-561, DEC-564; tests item_words::tests::the_undo_of_an_add_keeps_a_changed_entry, `item.spec.ts` "one add at a time, and the add toast's Undo keeps an entry that was changed meanwhile"
DEC-569 | Review OT2B-1 (high): a refetch of the item page rendered an open log dialog anew (focus to the panel, scroll position and the text typed into "Add a person" lost, focus on the body after closing, a second funnel intent). Where do the dialogs live? | Both dialogs are mounted once in `ItemPage`, after the `Transition`, next to `TagEditor` and `ListPicker`: always, not keyed and not conditional. What they know about the item is a page-owned signal (`ItemUi.info: RwSignal<Option<ItemInfo>>`, `play_title`) that every render of `ItemView` writes; the dialogs read it untracked when they open (reset, verb, board game, the "again" line) and when they save (item id, rating, note). The description under the title is a memo over the item's title (`FormDialog.description` became a `MaybeProp<String>`). Deviation from the review's sketch (a mount keyed on item id, model, verb, title): an unconditional mount renders the same nodes on the server and at hydration whatever arrives first (a closed dialog renders nothing), and it needs no key to be kept complete. In its place: when the route's item id changes the page closes an open dialog without asking (`dismiss`), as leaving the page does; the next opening resets the form for the new item. Focus back: the buttons that open the dialogs have the ids `ip-log-with` / `ip-log-play` and the dialogs name them as `return_to`, so after a refetch replaced the button that opened the dialog, focus goes to the one rendered in its place (also when the watched dialog was opened from an episode's menu, whose item is gone). `funnel::sheet_flow` is registered once per page: one opening is one intent. Correction to the review's scenarios: a change of the entry from elsewhere (tag, status, rating by API or another tab) does not refetch the item page, because `item.rs` matches `entity.updated` on the item id and the library publishes the entry id; only `catalog_item` events (enrichment of a stub, catalog refresh) and the page's own refreshes (300 ms after a save, a rating, a status) do. A save therefore causes one refetch, not two. Not changed: after Save the focus returns to the opener and the refetch that follows the save replaces that button, as after every one-click step of the page (the keyed view; older than this package) | key the mount on the item as the review sketched (a second source of truth for what is "fixed for an item"; a `Some`/`None` switch outside the `Transition` that the server and the client can see in a different state); defer refetches while a dialog is open (does not cover a fetch already under way, review 2.); keep the dialogs in the view and restore focus and scroll after each render (the typed text and the screen reader's place are still lost) | move the two components back into `ItemView` with `info=info.clone()`, drop `ItemUi.info`, `play_title`, `dismiss` and the two ids | review docs/reviews/2026-11-11-ot2b-review.md OT2B-1; HANDOFF 0 (no test workarounds for app races); WCAG 2.2 SC 2.4.3; tests `item.spec.ts` "a refetch of the page leaves an open log dialog alone: ..." and "another item opened while a log dialog is open closes the dialog; ..." (both seen red on the reviewed build), the `toPass` is gone from "the log dialogs are centered ..."; `ui/tests/render.rs` a_dialog_is_described_by_its_description_only_when_it_has_one
DEC-570 | Review OT2B-2 (medium): in a low window (320 x 256 CSS px: 1280 x 1024 at 400 %, WCAG 1.4.10) the body of a `FormDialog` shrank to nothing between header and footer, most of all with the discard question or the errors in the footer | As the review suggests: the panel scrolls as a whole when it is too low, and only then. `FORM_PANEL` got `overflow-y-auto`, `FORM_BODY` a floor of 8 rem (`min-h-32` for `min-h-0`). In a window of normal height nothing changes: the body shrinks first and scrolls inside, header and footer stay. Below the floor the footer is no longer fixed and moves with the panel, so it still covers no field (SC 2.4.11). A form shorter than 8 rem would get empty room below it; neither log dialog is that short | a floor only on phones (the case is a zoomed desktop window as well); let the footer scroll inside the body below a height (two layouts to test); `max-h-[100dvh]` for low windows (a little more room, the body is still gone with the question) | set `FORM_PANEL` and `FORM_BODY` back | review OT2B-2; DEC-566; WCAG 2.2 SC 1.4.10, 2.4.11; tests `item.spec.ts` "in a low window (320 x 256) the fields of a log dialog stay in reach, with the discard question and with errors" (seen red: the first field in view at a ratio of 0.71 with the question), `ui/tests/render.rs` form_dialog_scrolls_as_a_whole_when_the_window_is_too_low_for_its_body

DEC-572 | ot2c (OT-17 step 1, DEC-542): where do the per-kind totals come from, and how are seconds turned into minutes so that the tiles add up to the hero figure? | `App::stats_totals` keeps the kind when it folds the cache rows (`MonthAmounts`, one row per kind and month; no new query, no SQL change, no migration) and answers `kinds: Vec<KindTotals>` next to `overall` and `groups`: the kinds with activity, sorted by kind id. Seconds become whole minutes per kind; a group is the sum of its kinds and `overall` the sum of the groups, so tiles, group rows and the hero figure add up exactly. Before, seconds were divided per group; only the timed listening kinds carry seconds that do not fill a minute, so a group can now be lower by less than one minute per kind beyond the first. Kinds of the group Other are left out together with that group (no entries). The time series and the year comparison still divide per bucket; they are not shown next to the tiles as a sum. DEC-573 | ot2c: how is time shown, and what replaces "Minutes"? | Hours everywhere on Home and the Stats page: `Measure::Hours` (the amount stays minutes; `hours_of` gives one decimal below ten hours and whole hours from ten, through the locale formatter: "1.5 hours", "1,284 hours", "1,5 Stunden") leads the measures of Watch, Listen, Play and All (`measures_for`); Read has no time and keeps Pages first. It is used by the totals row of a tab, the column chart (values in minutes, axis ticks nice in hours: `axis`, `ColumnChart unit`), the chart's sentence and table, and the "year so far" comparison (on the All tab a group shows its first measure that has anything, so a library without runtimes keeps its "Watched" line). The words are written out ("312 hours", not "312 h"): the visible text is the text equivalent and reads the same in a screen reader. Goals are not touched: their measure "Minutes" keeps its name and its minute targets (`Measure::Minutes` only names a goal now) | "h" as an abbreviation with an `abbr` or `aria-label`; minutes below one hour; converting the goals to hours (changes stored targets) | `measures_for` and `Measure::Hours` in `crates/web/src/charts.rs` | ux-owner-test.md 4.2 row 3, 4.5 ("hours with one decimal below 10, whole above"); `crates/web/src/charts.rs` tests

DEC-581 | ot2d (OT-13): where do the accent and the tints go in the components, and where not? | Accent (`primary`, automatically violet in the default brand): filled buttons, `ButtonVariant::Link`, pressed chips, steps, switches and checkboxes, the default badge (the status pill), rating stars, the unread dot, chart bars of all groups together, and newly: progress bars (were `foreground`), the text of the selected segment and tab (on the unchanged `background` chip), the active navigation item (accent text; on `primary-soft` in the top bar and the side navigation), the "next up" episode row and the hero tile (`primary-soft`; the hero icon in the accent on a `background` disc). Group tints through `ui::Tint` (classes that name tokens only) and `web::tint`: the kind badge of the item page (soft surface, normal text, tinted icon), the kind icon of History rows, the four time tiles (soft surface, the kind's icon in the tint, text in the normal colours; movies and shows = watch, books = read, games = play), a marker dot before each group in the group switchers (Library, History, Stats, search scope) and before each line of Home's "Library" list, and the chart bars on a group's Stats tab. Not coloured: page, cards, borders, body text, field borders, focus rings (zinc), alerts and status dots. Three deliberate limits: (1) plain text links keep the foreground colour and their underline, only link buttons take the accent, because inline links also sit on alert and soft surfaces whose pairs the start-up check does not cover; (2) the tooltip becomes neutral (`foreground` / `background`), it was dark only because the old primary was dark; (3) library cards and Home rows carry no kind badge or icon today, so there is nothing to tint; the kind chips of the Library stay uncoloured until pressed (then accent). Title links that underline on hover only (item, list and person names in rows and cards) stay in the foreground colour: they are headings of their rows, not inline links, and a page of violet titles would be the loudest thing on it. `ButtonVariant::Link` is unchanged (accent) | colour every link including those in alerts (unchecked pairs; violet on amber-50 or red-50 is not in the check); add `primary` on the four status surfaces to the check and colour all (four more pairs per theme a brand must satisfy); a CSS rule on `a.underline` instead of the class at each site (a selector keyed to a utility name) | remove `text-primary` from the 38 `text-primary underline` class strings in `crates/web/src` | DEC-540; DEC-581; ux-owner-test.md 3.1; review docs/reviews/2026-11-11-ot2d-review.md OT2D-5; Playwright `home.spec.ts` "colour: ..." (link on the page) and `sources.spec.ts` "the Home nudge ..." (link on an alert); gallery section "Tints"
DEC-585 | Review ot2d, OT2D-3: the colour guard of DEC-583 only found six palette names. What does it catch now, and what is allowed? | Supersedes the scope of DEC-583. `xtask::whitelabel::colour_literal` reads every line (comment lines excepted) of `crates/ui/src`, `crates/web/src` and `apps/server/src` and reports: a class of any of the 22 Tailwind palette families followed by a shade (also zinc, red, emerald, amber, sky: component code names `success`, `muted` and so on); `black` or `white` on a colour utility; an arbitrary colour value on a colour utility (`bg-[#7008e7]`, `text-[oklch(...)]`, `bg-[red]`; lengths, `calc()`, `url()` and `var(--token)` pass); a hex colour of 3, 4, 6 or 8 digits (a fragment link `href="#add"` and a character reference are not colours); the functions `rgb(`, `rgba(`, `hsl(`, `hsla(`, `hwb(`, `lab(`, `lch(`, `oklab(`, `oklch(`, `color-mix(`; a literal in the SVG attributes `fill`, `stroke`, `stop-color`, `flood-color`, `lighting-color`; and an inline style that gives a colour property a literal (`style="..."`, `style=format!("...")`, Leptos `style:prop="..."`). The allow-list is `currentColor`, `none`, `transparent` and `var(--token)`, nothing else and no path exceptions. The stylesheet (`src/web/style`) keeps the rule of DEC-583: no palette family the brand colours come from; its zinc fallbacks, the four dark status surfaces (hex) and the shadows (`rgb()`) are its job.
