DEC-587 | ot2e, first task (DEC-537: not verified by the UX pass): how does a rewatch run behave through sync clients, the offline queue stamps (DEC-455) and the importers, and what marks a run? | Trace, read from the code on 2026-11-11. (1) Today's rule (`app/src/library.rs::log_progress_stamped`): only `finished` moves a status to Completed and only Planned moves to In progress; the move is stored on the event as `status_change` and applied with LWW on the stamp (the server's, or the client's `hlc` of a queued log, DEC-455); an episode logged on a Completed entry carries no move. No event today carries Completed -> In progress or, on an episode, -> Completed, so both are free to mean "run". (2) The plan's marker (ux-owner-test.md 1.4: a flag `rewatch_run` on the log call; the first rewatched episode carries Completed -> In progress) does not cover the menu's "Start a rewatch": that start has no episode to carry the move, and a status set by hand writes no event. Without one more stored bit a run with no episode yet ("Rewatch: 0 of 24", next step S1 E1) cannot be told from a show that is up to date and In progress. (3) Choice: the start is its own event, `Progress::Rewatch` (`{"type":"rewatch"}`, episodic model only, payload of the existing jsonb column: no migration, no new column). Logged on a Completed entry it carries `status_change` Completed -> In progress; on any other status it carries none and starts nothing. Derived from the effective history in its order (`occurred_at`, id): the run = the newest start that carries the move, its episodes = the regular episodes logged after it; it is finished when one of them carries -> Completed (`log_progress_stamped` writes that move on the event that makes the run cover every known regular episode, only while the entry is In progress), running while unfinished and the entry is In progress, stopped while unfinished and the entry is Completed (stop = the existing status write; no new call). Every episode logged while a run is running belongs to it, so no per-event flag and no `rewatch_run` field on `NewProgressDto`. (4) Sync clients: nothing changes for a client that does not know the type: its episode logs on a Completed entry stay extra views; it sees the start in the history like it sees `undo`; the status moves reach it as `library_entry` changes. A client starts a run with the existing `LogProgress` command; no new command type. (5) Offline queue (DEC-455): the start is queued as a log with the queue's stamp, the episodes after it in queue order; a replay is a no-op (op ids). The moves are LWW like every log's move: a status set later on another device wins. Then the start's move is on the event but not on the entry; with a later Completed that reads as a stopped run, with a later Paused or Dropped the run does not show (and no episode finishes it), with a later In progress it runs. A start that reaches an entry which is no longer Completed carries no move: its episodes are extra views. A stop queued after the start has the later stamp and wins. (6) Undo (DEC-208): undoing the start gives Completed back while the entry still carries that write, and the run is gone from the effective history; undoing the finishing episode reopens the run. (7) Importers: `importers/src/runner.rs` creates the entry with the file's status, logs the history through `log_progress` and sets the file's status last. Trakt and legacy rows never contain a start, so an import never starts a run and none of them changes (`crates/importers` untouched). Imported views are dated in the past: logged while a run is running they sort before its start and are not counted in it (membership is by history order, not by recording time), so an import cannot finish a run. The legacy "In progress with earlier finishes" entries (DEC-354) stay what they are today ("Up to date" or plain progress); they get no start because the file does not say when the rewatch began. The generic export and import carry the start like any other event (`history[].progress`). (8) Not a run: a movie (the single model refuses the type; a second `finished` on Completed moves nothing, as before); "Again" on one episode outside a run; a start on a show that is In progress (also an up-to-date one: the app cannot give Completed back at the end of what was never Completed; ux-owner-test.md 1.2 rule 6 offered the start there too). (9) Side effects accepted: the start is a row in the history and the activity lists and counts as one logged event in the stats (`events`); the episode that finishes a run counts as a completion in the stats and goals (`status_change ->> 'to' = 'completed'`), a stopped run does not. No SQL changes. | (a) the plan's flag on the first episode only, the menu arming the page until that episode (the armed state lives in one browser tab, the pill stays Completed after "Start"); (b) a column on `library_entries` (`rewatch_since`: a migration, a field in the sync entry and its own LWW rule; membership by time, so an import during a run would count); (c) a sixth status "Rewatching" (rejected in ux-owner-test.md 1.5: touches the approved status set, filters, stats, importers); (d) deriving a run from "In progress with a whole pass before the status stamp" (a status set back by hand for a new season would read as a rewatch) | remove `Progress::Rewatch` and the two moves in `log_progress_stamped`; stored starts then fail to parse, so first delete them (`DELETE FROM progress_events WHERE progress ->> 'type' = 'rewatch'`) or keep the variant unused | DEC-537; DEC-538; DEC-455; DEC-208; DEC-354; docs/fable/round2/ux-owner-test.md 1.2, 1.4; docs/fable/round2/next-packages.md ot2e; crates/app/src/library.rs; crates/api/src/sync.rs; crates/web/src/offline.rs; crates/importers/src/runner.rs, trakt.rs, legacy.rs
DEC-588 | ot2e: the Undo of "Log N again" takes a whole rewatch run back (its episodes newest first, then its start). The undo of the finishing episode restores In progress with a new stamp, so the undo of the start no longer found "exactly its write" on the entry (DEC-208) and left the show In progress. How does an undo recognise a write that another undo restored? | Two additions to the stored move, no migration (the `status_change` jsonb): (1) every `StatusChange` records `from_stamp`, the stamp of the write it replaced (absent on older events); (2) an undo stores the move it caused on its own event (`from` = what it took back, `to` = what it restored, its new stamp), like every other event does. `StatusChange::carried(entry stamp, write, history)`: the entry carries a write when its stamp is that write's, or when its stamp was written by an undo whose target had replaced that write (`from_stamp`), through any number of such undos. `App::undo_progress` uses it (`status_to_restore`). A status chosen by hand in between still stays: it has a stamp no undo wrote. Undo events are not part of the effective history, so stats, goals and the run derivation do not read their move. Test `app/tests/rewatch.rs::undoing_a_whole_run_gives_completed_back` (red: `in_progress` instead of `completed`; then green, with the hand-set case in the same test) | restore the old stamp with the old status (breaks LWW: clients that saw the newer stamp keep the newer value); let the start's undo set Completed whenever the entry is In progress (overwrites a status chosen by hand); undo the start first in the toast (the status would end In progress -> Completed -> ... in the wrong order and the finishing episode's undo would then find nothing to restore) | drop `from_stamp`, write `status_change: None` on undo events and compare `entry.status.stamp == c.stamp` in `app/src/library.rs` as before | DEC-208; DEC-587; crates/domain/src/progress.rs; crates/app/src/library.rs
DEC-589 | ot2e: choices made while building the rewatch run (model A, DEC-537; marker and rules in DEC-587) | (1) Where it starts: the overflow menu of a Completed show has "Start a rewatch" first (podcast: "Start listening again"); toast "Rewatch started" with Undo. The answer "Log N again" of the bulk question starts a run when the entry is Completed: the start, then the episodes, all dated now whatever the date choice says (review OT2A-10); on any other status the answer logs extra views as in ot2a. (2) While it runs: pill In progress; the line "Rewatch: 5 of 24 episodes. Watched once before."; the next step, the progress words and the bar on cards count within the run; the Episodes tab shows the run (what it reached is watched, the rest reads "Watched"/"Mark" again) and hides the date choice of the bulk marks (a backfilled view sorts before the start and would not count). The next step is the episode after the highest of the run; when that was the last known one, the first episode the run skipped. (3) Stop: the menu item for Completed reads "Stop the rewatch" while a run runs (one item, the same status write; the status select stops it too); the toast is the usual "Marked as Completed". Afterwards "Watched once; rewatch stopped after 5 of 24 episodes"; a run stopped without an episode says nothing. (4) Finish: the episode that makes the run cover every known regular episode sets Completed; its toast's second line is "Completed again: <title>" (the wording of ux-owner-test.md 1.3, "Rewatch finished. Watched 2 times.", was not taken: the toast does not know the count, the header shows it after the refetch). `ProgressEventDto.status` (additive) names the status an event moved the entry to. (5) The count (`app::times`): a run counts once. For a show it is the larger of the whole passes over the known episodes and "1 + runs that are over" (a run starts on a Completed entry, so there was a time before it; this also counts right for a show completed by hand with part of it logged). (6) Review OT2A-7: `ItemPageDto.times_at` is the day of the pass the count names (last finish; last play; for a show the day its last whole pass was done or its last run finished), and the count line uses it instead of the newest view; a completion by hand has no day ("Watched once"). (7) Not offered: a run on a show that is not Completed (DEC-587 (8)). (8) Wording per verb only where it must differ: podcasts get "listening again" for the menu, the start toast and the two lines; the finish toast and the history row ("Started again from the beginning") are neutral. Known limits, not built: a run that is running when the library is exported comes back without its start taking effect on re-import (the row is In progress when the start is replayed; the views are all there); the question of the bulk mark still says "as a rewatch" where the answer logs extra views (In progress shows); two logs at the same moment could both miss finishing a run (the pages then treat a run that covers every episode as over, and the status stays In progress until the person sets it) | start only from the bulk question (no way to start before the first episode); keep the full watched state in the list while a run runs (the list could not say where the rewatch is); a separate "stop" call and toast | per item: remove the menu item in `web/src/item.rs::overflow`; `again_starts_run` returns false; `season_rows_in` ignores the start; `fact_line` without the two run branches; `times` without `run_passes`; `fact_line` back to `summary.last_at` | DEC-537; DEC-538; DEC-564 (OT2A-7, OT2A-10); DEC-587; docs/fable/round2/ux-owner-test.md 1.2, 1.3; tests in crates/app/tests/rewatch.rs, crates/web/src/item_words.rs, episode_plan.rs, next_step.rs, apps/server/tests/it/item_page.rs, offline.rs, src/web/tests/item.spec.ts
DEC-590 | Review ot2e, OT2E-2 (must): a stop left no trace, so a stopped rewatch run came back with a later In progress and kept collecting views. What ends a run? | Orchestrator decision (the owner may reverse it): option (1) of the review. Stopping writes a marker, `Progress::RewatchStop` (`{"type": "rewatch_stop"}`, episodic model only, a payload of the existing jsonb column like the start: no migration, no new column). Logged while a run is open (its newest start has neither a finishing episode nor a stop marker) it carries In progress -> Completed like the finishing episode does (LWW on the stamp, DEC-455), and the run closes there: `domain::rewatch::runs` puts no later episode into it, `Run::phase` answers Stopped whatever the status says, so a later In progress by hand (a new season) shows no run, and views after the stop are plain extra views. Without an open run the stop is refused (`conflict`) and leaves no row. Undo of the stop reopens the run (DEC-208, DEC-588). The web's "Stop the rewatch" logs the marker (toast "Rewatch stopped" with Undo; offline it is queued as a log); the status select and the bulk status still write the plain status. A status write without the marker (an older client, the select) keeps the behaviour of DEC-587: the run reads as stopped while the entry is Completed, runs again with In progress, and collects later episodes until the next start or stop marker; this is kept on purpose and pinned by `a_run_stops_and_another_one_starts` (domain) and `undoing_a_stop_reopens_the_run` (app). The stop is one more event in `events` of the stats, like the start, and is not a completion: the three SQL statements that count `status_change ->> 'to' = 'completed'` leave `rewatch_stop` out (`store-pg/src/repos/library.rs`, `goals_groups.rs`, `stats_cache.rs`; `.sqlx` regenerated) and so does `app/src/stats.rs`. This replaces the wording of DEC-587: (3) "stopped while unfinished and the entry is Completed (stop = the existing status write; no new call)" now holds for a status write without the marker only; (4) and (8) "episode logs on a Completed entry stay extra views" hold after a stop marker and before any run, not after a plain status write; (5) a stop queued after the start is the marker. docs/modules/ROOT/pages/sync.adoc, section "Rewatch runs", says the same. Export and import: the markers are in the file, two stopped runs stay two (DEC-593) | option (2) of the review: a run is running only while the entry still carries the start's status write, any status chosen by hand ends it (no new data, but no pause and resume of a run, and views after a stop and the export stay wrong); a flag on the start type (`{"type": "rewatch", "end": true}`: one variant, but every match on the start would have to look at the flag); a status write that also appends the marker on the server (the plain select would then end a run for good and "pause" by status would be gone) | remove `Progress::RewatchStop`, `Run.stopped` and the stop item of `web/src/item.rs::RunMenu`; delete stored markers first (`DELETE FROM progress_events WHERE progress ->> 'type' = 'rewatch_stop'`), else they fail to parse | docs/reviews/2026-11-11-ot2e-review.md OT2E-2; DEC-587; DEC-589; tests domain `rewatch::tests::{a_stopped_run_does_not_come_back_with_in_progress, views_after_a_stop_do_not_join_the_run, two_stopped_runs_stay_apart, undo_takes_a_start_a_finish_and_a_stop_back}`, app `tests/rewatch.rs::{a_stop_marker_ends_the_run_for_good, undoing_a_stop_reopens_the_run}`, store-pg `phase1::stats_on_postgres_match_the_in_memory_rules`, server `it` `offline::a_rewatch_run_started_offline_replays_once`, `item_page::a_rewatch_run_shows_in_the_header_the_next_step_and_the_list`, `item.spec.ts` (all seen red first except the two `it` tests and the browser test, which were changed with the code)
DEC-591 | Review ot2e, OT2E-1 (must) and OT2E-12: a view dated before the start of a running rewatch run was logged and not counted; a start that started nothing was stored as a history row; a start could be dated before the first run's views. What does the server take? | The rules live in `app/src/run_rules.rs` and are checked in `App::log_progress` before anything is written, so the REST call, the sync command (offline queue) and "watched with" obey them; the web is not relied on. (1) While a run is running, an episode of a regular season whose `occurred_at` lies before the start of the run is refused (422 `invalid`, field `occurred_at`). The "Watched with..." dialog refuses the same before it sends: "You started again on <date>. Pick that day or a later one." (en, de), inline at the date chips; a day equal to the day of the start is sent with the start's own moment when noon would lie before it. Specials (season 0) are not part of a run and keep their dates. (2) An episode sent without a date while a run is running is dated `max(now, start + 1 ms)`: a start that came from a queue whose clock runs ahead (up to `CLOCK_SKEW_MINUTES`) no longer puts the next episode before itself. The same floor holds for a start and a stop sent without a date (not before the newest event). (3) OT2E-12, chosen: refuse, do not store. A start on an entry that is not Completed, or on an item that is not in the library (a marker never adds the item), and a stop without an open run answer 409 `conflict`; the offline queue shows it as a refused command like any other. (4) OT2E-12, `occurred_at` on a start, chosen: refuse the bad one, keep the rest. A start dated before the newest event of the item is refused (`invalid`): the views after it would become its run ("Watched 2 times" after one pass). The date is not ignored altogether because a queue sends the start with its own time and its episodes with later ones; a start re-dated to the flush would sort after its own episodes. A stop dated before its run's start is refused the same way. (5) A replay (same op id, DEC-455) answers the stored event before any rule is checked, so a flush sent twice stays a no-op. (6) A source that dates its views itself is the exception to (1): `App::import_progress` stores a view dated before a running run's start (it is old on purpose and is not counted in the run, DEC-587 (7)); the importers and the play of an integration (a media server's webhook with its own `watched_at`) use it, so a late scrobble is not answered with an error. (7) "Watched with" logs its session first: `App::check_progress` asks the same rules before, so a refused view leaves no session. One more indexed read per marker (the entry); episodes on an In progress entry read the history once, as before (OT2E-14, accepted) | (a) of the review only in the web (a script or the queue would still lose views); (b) log it and say in the toast that it does not count (the person still cannot finish the run); move the start back to the earliest view (changes a stored event, D-12); ignore `occurred_at` on a start (breaks the order of a queue, see (4)); store a start that starts nothing and hide it in the history (still an event in the stats) | drop the three arms of `run_rules::occurred_at` (it then answers `sent.unwrap_or(now)`), `App::check_progress` in `api/src/ops.rs::log_progress`, and `log_form::within_run` | docs/reviews/2026-11-11-ot2e-review.md OT2E-1, OT2E-12; DEC-587; DEC-455; tests app `tests/rewatch.rs::{a_view_dated_before_the_start_of_a_running_run_is_refused, a_start_cannot_be_dated_before_the_newest_view, what_is_not_a_run}`, api `rest::a_rewatch_run_refuses_what_it_could_not_count`, web `log_form::tests::a_day_before_the_start_of_a_running_rewatch_is_refused`, `log_sheet::tests::a_day_before_a_rewatch_names_the_day_it_started` (all seen red first), `item.spec.ts`
DEC-592 | Review ot2e, OT2E-5 and OT2E-6: DEC-588 changed when an undo reverts a status move (also when a later undo restored that write). Does that change undo outside a rewatch run, and is it tested? | It does not, and cannot: the chain rule (`StatusChange::carried`) differs from "exactly that write" (DEC-208) only when two events of one entry both moved the status and the later one is undone first. Outside a run no kind has two such events: `finished` is refused for a paged, timed, episodic or sessions kind (`Progress::validate_for`), and for the single model the first `finished` goes straight to Completed. The review's example (a book: a page log, then "Mark as finished", both undone) is not reachable; a book is completed by hand, which writes no event. Inside runs the chain can span several: the undo of a second run's start restores the Completed that the first run's last episode wrote, so that episode can still be undone (the first run runs again) and then the first start. A stop marker (DEC-590) is one more link of the same kind. Tests: `app/tests/undo.rs::an_undo_chain_needs_two_moving_events_and_only_runs_have_them` (the book refuses `finished`, its two page logs undo to Planned as before; two runs taken back newest first); four unit tests of `carried` in `domain/src/progress.rs` (the very write; through two undos; an event stored before `from_stamp` ends the chain; a stamp no undo wrote carries nothing older). They pin existing code and were green from the start. OT2E-6, second half: the domain test of undo no longer asserts a status its own helper computed; `Show::undo` appends the undo event only, the test checks what `runs` reads and names the app test as the one for the status | a hand-written completion event for books so that the review's case exists (a new event type, out of scope: see DEC-594, OT2E-3) | - (tests and wording only) | docs/reviews/2026-11-11-ot2e-review.md OT2E-5, OT2E-6; DEC-588; DEC-208
DEC-593 | Review ot2e, OT2E-7 a, OT2E-8 and OT2E-2 c: the generic export wrote undo events the import refuses (one undone bulk rewatch made the row of that show fail), no test carried a run through export and import, and a run that was running at export time came back without its start. What is in the file, and what does the import do with a run? | (1) The export writes the effective history (`App::history`): an undone log and its undo event are not in the file. This changes what an export contains; nothing a person sees in the app is missing from it. The import keeps refusing the undo type (a file is not a way to undo someone's events). (2) The markers of a run (`rewatch`, `rewatch_stop`) are rows of `history[].progress` like every event. The importer logs through `App::import_progress` (DEC-591 (6)), and before it replays a start it sets the entry to Completed: a start was only ever stored on a Completed entry, so this is the status the entry had, whatever the file's own status says at the end (it still has the last word). With that a run that was running at export time is running after the import; the limit DEC-589 listed is gone. (3) A marker the target account cannot take (it already holds newer views of the item, or the file is not in order) is left out and the row goes on: its views are still imported, the run is not. Trakt and legacy files never contain a marker and import as before. Test `api/tests/rest.rs::export_then_import_keeps_rewatch_runs_and_leaves_undone_logs_out` (two stopped runs in a row, a finished run with an undone extra view, a running run; the other account reads the same status, count, run and number of events; seen red: the file contained `"undo"`). OT2E-7 b: `web/src/offline.rs::queued_log` is now the one place that builds a queued log (the start and the stop of a run, the episodes of a bulk mark); test `offline::tests::a_rewatch_run_is_queued_as_logs_in_their_order`; the queue's replay on the server is `it` `offline::a_rewatch_run_started_offline_replays_once`. The async branches themselves (`log_run_marker`, `log_episodes` after a network failure) have no browser test: left for the offline pack. OT2E-7 c: domain `a_run_can_cover_every_episode_without_a_finishing_event`, app `a_run_that_covers_every_episode_is_over_without_a_finishing_event` | let the import accept undo events (it would have to map event ids of another account; `event_id` is not in the file); export `status_change` and replay it verbatim (the import would write statuses the rules did not derive) | `api/src/ops.rs::export_rows` back to `App::progress`; `importers/src/runner.rs::history` back to `log_progress` without the status write before a start | docs/reviews/2026-11-11-ot2e-review.md OT2E-7, OT2E-8, OT2E-2 c; DEC-587 (7); DEC-589 (limits)
DEC-594 | Review ot2e, the remaining findings: applied, deferred or accepted? | OT2E-3 deferred: "Completions" in stats and goals count events that moved an entry to Completed, so a show or a book completed by hand (no event) was never a completion, and a finished rewatch run now is one. This predates ot2e for first runs (0 before, 0 now). A fix needs a decision on what the measure means and an event for a status set by hand; follow-up for the stats package (ot2f or ot2g), not built here. Until then DEC-587 (9) stands: a finished run counts, a stopped one does not (DEC-590). OT2E-4 recorded as a limit of DEC-589: an undo of one episode inside a finished run, or of its start alone, leaves the run finished (the finishing event is still in the effective history: the count, the status and the completion stay). Checking a finished run against the episodes known today would be wrong the other way (a new season would un-finish it). OT2E-9 and OT2E-14 accepted as reviewed, no code; sync.adoc now says that `fraction` and `next_step` count within a running run. OT2E-10 applied: German keeps "beendet" for a finished pass and uses "abbrechen" / "abgebrochen" for a stop ("Wiederholung abbrechen", "... nach 5 von 24 Folgen abgebrochen"); the podcast stop reads "Stop this listen-through"; the finish toast is "Rewatch finished: <title>" / "Wiederholung beendet: <title>", for a podcast "Finished listening again: <title>" where the caller knows the kind (`LoggedStep.verb`; the palette and the cards read like a show). New: toast "Rewatch stopped" / "Wiederholung abgebrochen", history row "Stopped before the end" / "Vorzeitig abgebrochen". These replace the approved texts of ux-owner-test.md 1.3 for the stop: owner's call. OT2E-11 applied: while "Log N again" is asked on a Completed entry the date choice is hidden (`episode_plan::date_choice_shown`) and the question says "They are logged with today's date." OT2E-13 applied: `EpisodeList` is split as DEC-564 asked (`single_marks`, `BulkBar`, `DateChoice`; the component body is about 30 lines), and the two rewatch items of `item.rs::overflow` are built by `RunMenu::item` | OT2E-3: count a hand-set Completed in the three SQL statements from `library_entries` (no date to bucket it by); OT2E-10: keep the approved German texts | per item: the Fluent keys `item-rewatch-*`, `progress-rewatch-stop`, `episodes-again-now`; `date_choice_shown` returns `!in_run` | docs/reviews/2026-11-11-ot2e-review.md OT2E-3, 4, 9, 10, 11, 13, 14; DEC-564; DEC-587; DEC-589; tests web `item_words::tests::{a_rewatch_is_named_per_verb, the_fact_line_of_a_rewatch}`, `next_step::tests::the_toast_says_when_a_log_finished_a_rewatch`, `widgets::tests::progress_reads_as_words_in_each_locale` (seen red), `episode_plan::tests::the_date_choice_shows_only_where_it_has_a_say`
